### Mangaluru

#### Khader inspects DC office premises ahead of Cabinet meeting in M'luru

**In short:** Health and Family Welfare Minister U T Khader inspected the Dakshina Kannada Deputy Commissioner's office at Padil on Sunday ahead of a Cabinet meeting in Mangaluru on September 18, saying Chief Minister D K Shivakumar wanted it held at a government institution and that consultations with local MLAs and stakeholders would prepare coastal and Dakshina Kannada development proposals for Cabinet consideration. Khader defended the chief minister's aerial survey of drought-affected areas as giving a comprehensive picture of damage, urged the Opposition not to indulge in petty politics over disasters, and said the government had taken a clear stand on Nagendra's resignation on moral grounds.

#### PG student induction, orientation programme at YIASCM from today

**In short:** The Yenepoya Institute of Arts, Science, Commerce and Management will organise Navyarambh, a three-day PG student induction and orientation programme from September 7 to 9 at the YMK Auditorium, Kulur Campus, for newly admitted MBA, MCA, MSc Forensic Science, MSc Chemistry, MSc Microbiology, and M.Sc Food Science and Nutrition students. Dr Sreeramana Aithal, Director, Poornaprajna Institute of Management, Udupi, will be chief guest and Dr B T Nandish, Controller of Examinations, Yenepoya Deemed-to-be-University, guest of honour, with sessions on academic regulations, outcome-based education, career planning, professional ethics, digital learning, co-curricular activities and wellness.

#### Tourism Minister KJ George reviews M'luru port operations

**In short:** Karnataka Energy and Tourism Minister K. J.

Karnataka Energy and Tourism Minister K. J. George visited the New Mangalore Port Authority to inspect operational, infrastructural and tourism-related features, walking through cargo-handling areas and the Cruise Terminal before a briefing with Port Management. #### Walk-in interview on September 9

**In short:** Sahyog Multistate Credit Cooperative Society Ltd will hold a walk-in interview for vacant posts of Business Development Executive and Block Development Officer in Mangaluru on September 9 from 10 am to 1.30 pm at the District Employment Exchange Office in the District Administration Building at Padil. #### Forum offers reward of Rs 250 to public to report environmental violations

**In short:** The Society for Forest, Environment and Climate Change has announced Rs 250 for members of the public who share GPS-tagged photographs of environmental violations including improper waste disposal to WhatsApp number 9845490714, after which the Society will submit complaints in Form 1 to the Adjudicating Officer under Section 15C of the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986. Society secretary Benedict Fernandes cited RTI information alleging 4,73,684 offences recorded in 2024-25 with penalties of around Rs 79 crore collected versus an alleged short levy of approximately Rs 394 crore, and accused subordinate Karnataka authorities and pollution control boards of failing to follow amended civil penalty provisions ranging from Rs 10,000 to Rs 15 lakh.

#### Cleanliness drive held

**In short:** Ramakrishna Mission's Swachh Mangaluru Abhiyan cleanliness drive was flagged off at Central Library Park, Kadri Mallikatte, by MRPL-ONGC General Manager Mandar Kale and Biligiri Ranganathaswamy Grama Panchayat PDO Shashikala, with staff from BR Hills visiting to learn the Mission's practices. Students from Nitte Institute of Professional Education cleaned the library surroundings and Srinivasa Institute of Aviation cleaned the Saxophonist Kadri Gopalnath Memorial inside the park, removing waste, dry leaves, debris, moss and algae.

#### MCC Commissioner inspects infra projects

**In short:** Mangaluru City Corporation Commissioner Rahul Ratnam Pandey inspected the proposed multi-level car parking facility at the old bus stand on K S Rao Road under the PPP model, where MSCL General Manager Arun Prabha said around 84 landowners had not agreed to part with properties and the old bus stand is more than seven decades old with a proposal to lease land for 30 years. Pandey directed officials to submit a month-wise report for the next 15 months within a week, and at the Surathkal-Idya wet well he directed water samples be sent for laboratory testing after residents complained of groundwater contamination affecting local wells.

#### Spl package sought for Koraga rehabilitation, plastic-free DK

**In short:** MLC Kishore Kumar and Jana Shikshana Trust urged Chief Minister D K Shivakumar ahead of the September 18 Cabinet meeting in Mangaluru to provide a comprehensive Koraga rehabilitation package on Kerala-model lines including agricultural land, housing, education, healthcare and employment, declare Dakshina Kannada a plastic-free and open-dumping-free district, survey and conserve public lakes and wells, construct open wells where drinking water is inadequate, strengthen Maadari Vikasa Kendra with financial assistance, and set up an elephant camp in Gundya amid rising elephant menace in Sullia, Puttur, Kadaba and Belthangady.

#### Khader announces eye banks in all dist hqrs

**In short:** Health and Family Welfare and Minority Welfare Minister U T Khader called upon people to spread the message of eye donation at a rally organised by Prasad Netralaya Super Speciality Eye Hospital and the Dakshina Kannada District Health and Family Welfare Department, saying steps would be taken to establish eye banks at every district headquarters and the government would consider incentives for organ donors. He also stressed learning multiple languages beyond Kannada and English to broaden knowledge and employment prospects.

#### Minister George rules out casinos on state's coast

**In short:** Tourism Minister K J George said the government is studying cruise tourism and Kerala-style houseboats to raise coastal tourism to international standards, including a possible Mangaluru–Goa cruise with en-route stops and efforts to retain international cruise tourists at New Mangalore Port. Inspecting Tannirbhavi blue flag beach, he ruled out casinos on the Karnataka coast, said more beaches may be proposed for blue flag certification, directed resolution of closed food-stall revenue disputes, and said genuine homestays may offer up to six rooms while larger establishments need hotel licences.

#### MLA orders evening cop cover at Darbe Circle

**In short:** Puttur Congress MLA Ashok Rai has ordered a police arrangement near Darbe circle from 3 p.m. to 4.30 p.m., when St.

Puttur Congress MLA Ashok Rai has ordered a police arrangement near Darbe circle from 3 p.m. to 4.30 p.m., when St. Philomena College, the high school, Bethany School and Little Flower School let out and students scramble for buses amid moving traffic. After visiting the circle on Saturday evening and hearing from rickshaw drivers, shop owners and parents, Rai said personnel will be posted for children's safety and directed the municipality to shift the auto stand backward, demolish an old building there, and make provision for small stall-shops on the site.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** MLA orders evening cop cover at Darbe Circle
- **Told:** Puttur Congress MLA Ashok Rai has ordered a police arrangement near Darbe circle from 3 p.m.
- **Purpose:** After visiting the circle on Saturday evening and hearing from rickshaw drivers, shop owners and parents, Rai said personnel will be posted for children's safety and directed the municipality to shift the auto stand backward, demolish an old building there, and make provision for small stall-shops on the site.

**Important points**
- Philomena College, the high school, Bethany School and Little Flower School let out and students scramble for buses amid moving traffic.
- After visiting the circle on Saturday evening and hearing from rickshaw drivers, shop owners and parents.

#### NITK rolls out bold and balanced curriculum for 2026 B.Tech batch

**In short:** National Institute of Technology Karnataka, Surathkal, has introduced a new "bold and balanced" B.Tech curriculum from the 2026 batch, cutting overall credits while allowing optional Honours in the main discipline or a Minor in another department or interdisciplinary field. Classroom theory credits now nearly match practical credits beyond the classroom, so students will spend two-thirds of their time on labs, life-skills activities, industry internship, R&D projects and startup planning; deans said the three-year design, built with industry and alumni input, aims to ready graduates for the AI era with human values alongside technical skill.

#### Rise and reclaim' Assembly presses for rights, power

#### Nethravathi cruise, houseboats planned to give Mangaluru tourism a fresh push

**In short:** Tourism Minister K J George said Karnataka is planning Nethravathi river cruises and houseboats, plus an integrated coastal and Malnad tourism plan covering beaches, water sports and community-driven tourism, after inspecting the river, Tannirbhavi Blue Flag Beach and New Mangalore Port on Monday. George said the first phase of Tannirbhavi infrastructure should finish within six months, cruise-linked circuits would link NMPT passengers to coastal attractions, and the Mangaluru–Karwar–Goa stretch could expand with private operators, while CRZ clearances remain a challenge; he ruled out casino tourism, said basic infrastructure must precede water sports, and that genuine homestays would be encouraged but not commercial lodges posing as homestays.

#### Govt. intends to develop existing Blue flag beaches, says K.J. George

**In short:** Energy and Tourism Minister K.J. George said the State intends to fully develop existing Blue Flag beaches at Tannirbhavi rather than opening new ones, citing Coastal Regulation Zone norms as the biggest challenge for coastal tourism.

Energy and Tourism Minister K.J. George said the State intends to fully develop existing Blue Flag beaches at Tannirbhavi rather than opening new ones, citing Coastal Regulation Zone norms as the biggest challenge for coastal tourism. Chief Minister Shivakumar is serious about coastal and Malnad tourism; Tourism Secretary Ram Prasath Manohar is studying cruise tourism, shipyards and houseboats, while the government is ruling out casinos and considering keeping beaches open beyond 7 p.m. George visited Tannirbhavi Blue Flag and the main beach, said the Tannirbhavi shop owner revenue dispute will be resolved, homestays must follow policy, and the government supports private hospitals in Dakshina Kannada for health tourism.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Govt. intends to develop existing Blue flag beaches, says K.J. George
- **Told:** Energy and Tourism Minister K.J.
- **Purpose:** George visited Tannirbhavi Blue Flag and the main beach, said the Tannirbhavi shop owner revenue dispute will be resolved, homestays must follow policy, and the government supports private hospitals in Dakshina Kannada for health tourism.

**Important points**
- Chief Minister Shivakumar is serious about coastal and Malnad tourism.
- Tourism Secretary Ram Prasath Manohar is studying cruise tourism, shipyards and houseboats, while the government is ruling out casinos and considering keeping beaches open beyond 7 p.m.
- George visited Tannirbhavi Blue Flag and the main beach, said the Tannirbhavi shop owner revenue dispute will be resolved, homestays must follow policy, and the government supports private hospitals in Dakshina Kannada for health tourism.

#### Mangaluru Railway Region transition: Mysuru, Palakkad officials to meet this week

**In short:** Divisional Railway Managers of Mysuru (SWR) and Palakkad (SR) divisions will meet in Mangaluru on Thursday to finalise the transition of the Mangaluru Railway Region from Palakkad to Mysuru, effective October 1 per an August 21 Railway Ministry order. SWR jurisdiction will extend to Ullal (inclusive) from Padil and till Thokur (exclusive) north — about 30 km of the 39 km route to Mysuru — covering roughly 2,000 railwaymen; Mangaluru Central handles about 30 arrivals/departures and the Junction about 70 halting plus 10 originating trains, with Central housing a Palakkad coaching depot of about 17 pairs and ~450 coaches, and Panambur Yard handling NMPA freight.

#### High Court turns down appeal by insurance company

**In short:** Karnataka High Court upheld a Permanent Lok Adalat Mangaluru order directing National Insurance to reimburse ₹2,85,470 for post-chemotherapy injections Zoladex and Xgeva to Padmanabha Shetty G., 72, a retired Vijay Bank officer with Stage IV prostate cancer under the IBA group scheme. The insurer argued outpatient exclusion; the HC dismissed the writ petition on September 1, imposing ₹50,000 costs.

### Coastal Karnataka

#### Opposition to Sharavathi project being fuelled by vested interests: George

**In short:** Energy Minister K J George on Sunday alleged that opposition to the proposed Sharavathi pumped storage project was being fuelled by vested interests, including a politician trying to protect encroached land from which compensation would not be available if acquired, and dismissed claims that the project would threaten lion-tailed macaque habitat. Speaking at the silver jubilee of Jayashreekrishna Parisara Premi Samiti in Mangaluru, he said the project needs only around 125 acres—about 100 for pipelines that would be restored—with 10 saplings planted for every tree felled, while NRI entrepreneur Ronald Colaco called for a dedicated expert task force and said the coastal districts had not achieved even 10 per cent of their development potential despite abundant resources.

#### KSRTC bus catches fire

**In short:** A KSRTC Sarige bus travelling from BC Road to Bengaluru via Subrahmanya was gutted after catching fire near Kaikamba on the Kaikamba-Gundya road in Bilinele late on Saturday night, but all 30-35 passengers were evacuated safely after the BC Road depot crew noticed the fire and alerted them. KSRTC officials from the Puttur division inspected the spot and initiated an inquiry as the exact cause is yet to be established.

#### 'Lone jumbo being closely monitored'

**In short:** Forest Department officials are closely monitoring a lone elephant spotted near SDM Engineering College campus at Ujire on Sunday morning after it moved from the Uppinangady side towards Kalmanja and Dharmasthala. Belthangady Range Forest Officer Mahesh Devadiga said Elephant Task Force personnel and forest staff fired warning shots into the air to drive the animal away from human settlements, and the elephant has started moving towards the forest area as surveillance continues.

#### MAHE signs MoU with University of Sydney

**In short:** Manipal Academy of Higher Education and the University of Sydney signed a Memorandum of Understanding at the Australian Consulate-General Residence in Mumbai to strengthen collaboration in education, research, innovation and international mobility, covering flagship research programmes, joint research funding and doctoral education, student and faculty mobility, innovation and knowledge translation, and joint governance with measurable outcomes. MAHE Vice Chancellor Dr Sharath K Rao and University of Sydney Deputy Vice Chancellor (Research) Prof Mike Ryan signed the agreement in the presence of Australian High Commissioner Philip Green OAM and Consul-General Paul Murphy.

#### Facilitation centres to boost tourism in coastal region

**In short:** Karnataka's Tourism Department plans single-window facilitation centres to help investors and boost coastal tourism through wellness, cruise and boathouse tourism inspired by Kerala, including medium-scale affordable spas and boathouse projects pending KTCDA and Coastal Business Zone approvals. Tourism Secretary Ram Prasath Manohar said coastal areas are untouched opportunities where cruises from Mumbai to Lakshadweep via Mangaluru could be encouraged to keep passengers overnight, and a travel circuit mixing water sports is being created.

### Karnataka

#### K'taka to open govt doors for startups

**In short:** Startups in Karnataka may soon participate in state procurement and receive government work orders and support of up to Rs 25 lakh, following IT-BT Minister Priyank Kharge's announcement that the government will be the first customer of startups working on governance and public service delivery. The IT-BT department has drawn up specifics of the 'Government First' initiative—earmarking Rs 25 crore to support 100 startups over three years—and will place the proposal before the Cabinet, relaxing barriers such as prior experience, earnest money deposits and performance security while enabling departments to share datasets, space and problem statements with startups.

#### B'luru has 4,800-km-long problem

**In short:** As much as 4,800 km of Bengaluru's 10,000-km water supply pipelines were laid nearly 70-80 years ago, and 57 of the city's 83 Ground Level Reservoirs are 40-70 years old, causing frequent leakages, repeated repairs and road damage, a senior BWSSB official said. In the 240 sq km core area alone, 4,800 km of pipeline is 70-80 years old; another 2,400 km in seven CMCs and one TMC added in 2007 is 10-15 years old, and 2,800 km in 110 villages added to BBMP in 2007 is 1-2 years old, with old cast iron pipes in some cases allowing sewage to enter leaks and mix with water.

#### Nominations open

**In short:** Deccan Herald's Changemakers to Watch in 2027 awards invites nominations from readers starting today for 10 trailblazers from across Karnataka below age 45 whose work has inspired change in fields including community empowerment, health, entrepreneurship, the arts and conservation. Since the awards launched in 2019, more than 150 individuals have been recognised; the New Year's Day edition will feature the selected pioneers alongside docu-films, and the last date for submitting nominations is September 25, 2026.

#### Stay on socio-edu survey report should be lifted: Siddaramaiah

**In short:** Former chief minister Siddaramaiah urged lifting the stay on publicising Karnataka's socio-educational survey report ahead of a September 10 high court hearing on a KSCBC petition, saying he had dreamt of the survey since 1996 and accused former CM H D Kumaraswamy of rejecting the H Kantharaj-led backward classes commission report. He sought backward-class reservation in Assembly and Lok Sabha elections and hiking total reservation to at least 75%, while Deputy CM G Parameshwara hailed his Ahinda mobilisation and CM D K Shivakumar said Congress ideology is Ahinda ideology.

#### Medicines worth Rs 4L seized in FDA raids

**In short:** Karnataka's Food Safety and Drug Administration drugs enforcement wing on Sunday raided two unlicensed Amazon storage facilities in Anekal and Devanahalli and seized unauthorised medicines worth Rs 4 lakh following intelligence on compliance violations. At Thattanahalli, Anekal, medicines worth Rs 2.45 lakh were seized from Amazon Seller Services Pvt Ltd, and at Aerospace Park, Devanahalli, medicines worth Rs 1.57 lakh were seized.

#### AICCTU condemns remarks by Byre Gowda on pourakarmikas

**In short:** The All India Central Council Trade Unions (AICCTU) strongly condemned remarks by Bengaluru Development Minister Krishna Byre Gowda during a Saturday inspection of the waste management system in North City Corporation limits, when pourakarmikas requested regularization of employment and he asked how those who “don’t come to work” could be made permanent. AICCTU demanded an apology on Sunday, calling his behaviour objectionable and arrogant, and said civic workers are frontline workers who deserve respect, especially given that many are women and Dalits facing discrimination.

The All India Central Council Trade Unions (AICCTU) strongly condemned remarks by Bengaluru Development Minister Krishna Byre Gowda during a Saturday inspection of the waste management system in North City Corporation limits, when pourakarmikas requested regularization of employment and he asked how those who “don’t come to work” could be made permanent. AICCTU demanded an apology on Sunday, calling his behaviour objectionable and arrogant, and said civic workers are frontline workers who deserve respect, especially given that many are women and Dalits facing discrimination. The union also demanded regularization with weekly leave, legal holidays, timely wages, safety equipment, social security, job security, and an end to exploitation through contract and outsourcing.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** AICCTU condemns remarks by Byre Gowda on pourakarmikas
- **Told:** The All India Central Council Trade Unions (AICCTU) strongly condemned remarks by Bengaluru Development Minister Krishna Byre Gowda during a Saturday inspection of the waste management system in North City Corporation limits, when pourakarmikas requested regularization of employment and he asked how those who “don’t come to work” could be made permanent.
- **Purpose:** The union also demanded regularization with weekly leave, legal holidays, timely wages, safety equipment, social security, job security, and an end to exploitation through contract and outsourcing.

**Important points**
- The union also demanded regularization with weekly leave, legal holidays, timely wages, safety equipment, social security, job security, and an end to exploitation through contract and outsourcing.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.4; News Trail Mangaluru p.2_

#### Governor writes to Rayareddi for action against KSHEC vice-chairman

**In short:** Governor Thaawarchand Gehlot has written to Higher Education Minister Basavaraj Rayareddi asking him to initiate action against Karnataka State Higher Education Council (KSHEC) vice-chairman S R Niranjana, accused of misusing public funds. The governor termed the allegations grave, citing documents showing reimbursements from KSHEC funds for personal expenses including Vijayanagar Club bills totalling Rs 34,350 in June–July 2024, plus other claims of Rs 60,000, Rs 12,000, Rs 25,289 and Rs 50,213 between March and May 2025; the complainant also sought Niranjana's removal pending inquiry.

#### After political sabbatical, Siddu looks to reclaim Ahinda arena

**In short:** Former chief minister Siddaramaiah returned to the forefront of Ahinda (minorities, backward classes and Dalits) politics at Sunday's Mega Awareness Convention of Backward Castes at Bengaluru's Palace Grounds, where he was felicitated for his eight-year chief ministership (2013–18 and 2023–26). Political observers said his renewed activity could help Congress retain OBC and SC/ST support amid Chief Minister D K Shivakumar's Vokkaliga-led leadership, while analyst Harish Ramaswamy noted Siddaramaiah hopes for another Congress term in 2028 and has been tasked with mobilising OBCs nationally ahead of elections in Uttar Pradesh and Punjab.

#### Electoral system has become costly: Siddaramaiah

**In short:** Former chief minister Siddaramaiah on Sunday dismissed claims linking his potential electoral retirement to age, saying he would remain in active politics but not contest elections because the electoral system had become increasingly costly and needed reform. Speaking at the Karnataka State Federation of Backward Castes convention in Bengaluru, he said he had fought injustice since his student days and emotional followers urged him not to retire, with Chikkaballapur MLA Pradeep Eshwar calling him a future chief minister.

#### KPSC 'scam': CID hunts for another suspect

**In short:** The CID is searching for KPSC member Shanta Hosamani, at large since ED raids on August 18, with her phone switched off and two CID notices unacknowledged; the ED has issued a lookout notice at airports. Hosamani, appointed in 2021 for six years and named in an August 7 FIR under the Karnataka Public Examination Act, is allegedly linked to illegal gratification in recruitment, while the CID continued questioning suspended IAS officer Gyanendra Kumar Gangwar and alleged linchpin Basavaraj Kannale; former KPSC chairman Shivashankarappa S Sahukar denied links to Kannale and faces an anticipatory bail hearing on Monday.

#### BMW biker flees after fatal collision near Devanahalli

**In short:** A 25-year-old man, Naveen Kumar of Narganahalli in Devanahalli taluk, died on the spot early Sunday when a BMW RR motorcycle allegedly collided with the Maruti Swift Dzire he was travelling in near Chikkasannae gate signal; the BMW rider Rakesh, 28, of Peenya, abandoned the bike and fled. Police suspect speeding and negligent riding and are registering a case at Devanahalli traffic station while efforts continue to trace Rakesh.

#### California has 16L IT professionals, B'luru has 26L: DKS

**In short:** Chief Minister D K Shivakumar on Sunday said Bengaluru had 26 lakh IT professionals compared with 16 lakh in California's Silicon Valley, with 2 lakh foreign passport holders working in the city and over 1 lakh engineers and 13,940 doctors produced each year. Speaking at the Study Abroad 2026 programme, he said over 80 universities offered more than 300 subjects, with government scholarships and bank loans up to Rs 2 crore available for students.

#### Lack of amenities, neglected communities fuel Ch'nagar cauldron

**In short:** Official Forest Department data shows MM Hills Wildlife Sanctuary, proposed as Karnataka's sixth tiger reserve, accounted for 166 of 370 poaching cases (45%) and 32 of 66 gun cases in the Chamarajanagar circle between 2010–11 and 2026–27, with the circle registering 96 wildlife offence cases in 2024–25 including killings of five tigers and deaths of three cubs. Officials said poverty, lack of health and education amenities and neglected border communities drive poaching, and PCCF (Wildlife) Kumar Pushkar called for an integrated multi-department approach beyond patrols alone.

#### Gen Z's call for dissent, accountability at Gauri Day

**In short:** The Gauri Memorial Trust marked journalist-activist Gauri Lankesh's death anniversary as 'Gen Z Day' in Bengaluru on Sunday, with SFI national joint secretary Aishe Ghosh and CJP co-convener Ashutosh Ranka addressing shrinking democratic spaces, paper leaks and student uprisings. Ranka recalled the 37-day Jantar Mantar protests, citing satire, meme culture and a minimum common agenda as keys to mobilising millions, while trustees including Teesta Setalvad and Kavitha Lankesh were present.

#### Rowdy ordered to stay out of Bengaluru South district

**In short:** Chandra Shekar alias Chandra, a history-sheeter in his 30s with a history sheet at Kaggalipura police station accused in four Kaggalipura and two Thalaghattapura cases including murder and attempted murder, has been externed from Bengaluru South district after police said he continued criminal activities while on conditional bail. Bengaluru South Superintendent of Police Rohan Jagadeesh issued the externment order on September 3.

#### Depleting water in dams: Agri scenario grim in Mysuru region

**In short:** Declining water in Kabini and KRS reservoirs after releases to Tamil Nadu has left storage sufficient only for drinking water, with Mysuru district achieving just 44.11% of its 3,76,609-hectare sowing target (1,66,441 hectares) and the region expected to record less than 50% sowing overall. District in-charge Minister Yathindra Siddaramaiah has restricted releases to filling lakes and ponds, not paddy; Mandya farmers were told not to cultivate paddy while Hassan farmers were urged to grow ragi on 93,004 of 2,86,186 targeted hectares amid deficit rains.

#### KSPCB directs closure of Belagavi sugar factory for violations

**In short:** The Karnataka State Pollution Control Board has ordered Ugar Sugar Pvt Ltd in Ugar-Khurd, Athani taluk, Belagavi, to shut operations until further orders after repeated non-compliance despite hearings since March 2024, including failure to adopt zero liquid discharge, treat excess condensate and provide a seepage-proof lined pond. The closure notice followed activist Dinesh Kallahalli's complaint about delayed action; the Deputy Commissioner, Belagavi, was told to seize the industry and power supply was ordered cut off.

- **Purpose:** The closure notice followed activist Dinesh Kallahalli's complaint about delayed action; the Deputy Commissioner, Belagavi, was told to seize the industry and power supply was ordered cut off.

**Important points**
- The Karnataka State Pollution Control Board has ordered Ugar Sugar Pvt Ltd in Ugar-Khurd, Athani taluk, Belagavi, to shut operations until further orders after repeated non-compliance despite hearings since March 2024, including failure to adopt zero liquid discharge, treat excess condensate and provide a seepage-proof lined pond.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.6_

#### Tigress dies at Tyavarekoppa

**In short:** Dashami, a tigress born on October 16, 2009, who spent her entire life at Tyavarekoppa Tiger and Lion Safari in Shivamogga, died on Sunday morning of age-related illnesses after 10 days of illness under veterinary care; a post mortem will be performed at the Veterinary College, Shivamogga, said Executive Director K V Amarakshara.

#### Farmers oppose move to acquire land for industrial corridor

**In short:** Farmers in Hosaval, Veerapur, Ramapur, Heggeri and surrounding villages have opposed the government's notification to acquire around 6,000 acres for the Dharwad–Belagavi industrial corridor under the Suvarna Karnataka project, alleging about 270 acres they have cultivated for generations was reclassified as 'B kharab' government land. Protesting for five days, farmer leaders Uday Kumar and Mohammad Ali demanded at least Rs 1 crore per acre against the fixed compensation of Rs 45 lakh per acre.

#### 118 engineering officers pass out from AFTC B'luru

**In short:** The Air Force Technical College, Bengaluru, held the passing-out parade of the 107 Aeronautical Engineering Course on Saturday, graduating 118 engineering officers including five from foreign countries after 62 weeks of training reviewed by Air Marshal Yalla Umesh. Flying Officer Raghvendra Pratap Singh won the Sword of Honour, Flying Officer Vaibhav Mishra received the President's Plaque, and Flying Officers Rohan Veer Singh Rawat and Keshika K won Chief of Air Staff Medals for the Electronics and Mechanical streams.

#### Work launched to supply water to 12 taluks

**In short:** Scheduled Tribes Welfare Minister T Raghumurthy on Sunday launched work to supply Tunga and Bhadra waters to 12 taluks across four districts under the Upper Bhadra Project, with 12.9 tmcft from Bhadra dam and 17 tmcft from Tunga reservoir allotted to fill 367 tanks and irrigate 2.25 lakh hectares in Chitradurga, Davangere, Tumakuru and Chikkamagaluru districts through Visvesvaraya Jala Nigama Limited.

#### Startups to get govt's procurement push

**In short:** Karnataka's IT BT department will give startups a procurement push by enabling them to test, validate and demonstrate solutions in real-world government environments, with a selection committee headed by a domain expert as sole evaluation authority to assess feasibility, approve pilots and recommend successful solutions for procurement. The initiative supports procurement across goods, services and integrated solutions with flexibility for scaling across departments.

#### Idli-dosa batter to global fresh food: iD Fresh's next big menu

**In short:** Bengaluru-based iD Fresh Food plans to add 10,000 stores over 18 months, launch about one new SKU a month, expand fresh batter into the US, Canada and Europe, and target an IPO in November 2027 as it seeks Rs 2,000 crore revenue within three years. Co-founder PC Musthafa said the profitable company, which sells through around 60,000 outlets in India and recorded Rs 681 crore revenue in FY25, will fund expansion from internal accruals while investing Rs 35–40 crore in a new Mumbai factory and Rs 25 crore in a Dubai plant.

#### Double date with destiny awaits Bengaluru's Macarton

**In short:** Bengaluru-born midfielder Macarton Louis Nickson, picked in India's 26-man squad by coach Khalid Jamil, faces a homecoming against Panama in Bengaluru on September 25 followed by fixtures against Brazil on October 3 and Uruguay on October 6 in Kolkata. The 22-year-old, who made his senior India debut after his first call-up in 2025, grew up watching Neymar and says playing Brazil would be an unforgettable day, though he must compete with experienced midfielders including Apuia, Anirudh Thapa and Sahal Abdul Samad at the national camp starting September 14.

#### Karnataka men and under-19 boys stamp regional supremacy

**In short:** Karnataka's men's and boys' U-19 teams won the South Zone Inter-State Badminton Championships in Bengaluru on Sunday, the men's team edging Telangana 3-2 after rallying from 2-0 down and the U-19 boys defeating Telangana 2-0. Andhra Pradesh won the women's title 3-2 over Tamil Nadu, while Tamil Nadu took the girls' Under-19 crown 2-0 over Telangana; Kishal Ganapathy and Raghu Mariswamy sealed the men's deciding doubles 15-9, 15-13.

#### City's Zidaan, Akshat hot on wheels

**In short:** Bengalureans Zidaan Arshaan Anees and Akshat Misra dominated their classes at the fifth round of the MECO-FMSCI Indian National Karting Championships at Meco Kartopia on Sunday, each winning Pre-Final and Final in Micro Max and Senior Max respectively for Peregrine Racing. Ridhaan Anil Kumarr finished runner-up in Mini Max behind Faridabad's Arshi Gupta, who clocked eight consecutive fastest laps in the Final; Shivneel Karthikeyan, son of India's first Formula One driver Narain Karthikeyan, finished third in Mini Max.

#### Deshpande has Mangalore on the mat

**In short:** Atharva Deshpande took 7/23 as Secretary's XI gained first-innings lead over Mangalore Zone in a KSCA under-19 inter-zonal tournament match in Bengaluru, after Mangalore were bowled out for 106 in reply to Secretary's XI's 236 all out. #### 'Never thought of playing 100 first-class games'

**In short:** Karnataka leg-spinner Shreyas Gopal, playing his 100th first-class match in the Duleep Trophy final for South Zone, said he never imagined reaching the milestone when debuting as a 20-year-old against Mumbai in December 2013. Gopal finished last Ranji Trophy season as third-highest wicket-taker with 48 scalps at 23.14 and has 305 first-class wickets at 28.25, driven by a desire to prove leg-spinners can succeed in red-ball cricket.

#### How the Alpine Pipers bond off the board

**In short:** Alpine APL Pipers coach GM Pravin Thipsay says the Global Chess League team—featuring Magnus Carlsen, Koneru Humpy, Divya Deshmukh, Vidit Gujrathi, Anish Giri and Volodar Murzin—bonds through nightly strategy meetings and morning follow-ups where every player's opinion is heard, including the youngest members. The squad mixes flamboyant and introverted personalities off the board but fuses as one team on it, celebrating victories collectively and absorbing losses with encouragement during the fourth GCL edition in Bengaluru.

#### Carlsen survives; Sindarov succumbs

**In short:** Defending champions Alpine APL Pipers beat Ganges Grandmasters 11-6 on day two of the Global Chess League in Bengaluru, with Magnus Carlsen escaping defeat against Ian Nepomniachtchi and Koneru Humpy and Vidit Gujrathi scoring wins. Ganges Grandmasters later shocked overnight leaders FYERS American Gambits 9-7, PBG Alaskan Knights beat CheQ Mumba Masters 11-6, and Mumba Masters edged Triveni Continental Kings 10-9; Alpine Pipers, FYERS American Gambits and Ganges Grandmasters lead with six match points each.

#### London-bound plane makes 'emergency landing' at B'luru

**In short:** A London-bound Virgin Atlantic flight carrying 270 passengers was forced to make an emergency landing at Kempegowda Airport in Bengaluru on Sunday after developing a glitch after take-off, Bangalore International Airport Limited sources said. The glitch prompted the crew to circle near Tumakuru for more than half an hour before returning to Bengaluru.

#### Siddaramaiah demands 75% reservation for Dalits, BCs, minorities

**In short:** Former Karnataka Chief Minister Siddaramaiah on Sunday called for implementation of the caste census and increase of reservation for Dalits, backward communities and minorities to 75 per cent at the Karnataka State Backward Castes Federation's Silver Jubilee celebration in Bengaluru. He said the caste survey report submitted by the Backward Classes Commission headed by Madhusudan Naik, which he had accepted before demitting office, must be implemented but a court order currently prevents it from being implemented or made public.

Former Karnataka Chief Minister Siddaramaiah on Sunday called for implementation of the caste census and increase of reservation for Dalits, backward communities and minorities to 75 per cent at the Karnataka State Backward Castes Federation's Silver Jubilee celebration in Bengaluru. He said the caste survey report submitted by the Backward Classes Commission headed by Madhusudan Naik, which he had accepted before demitting office, must be implemented but a court order currently prevents it from being implemented or made public. Siddaramaiah said reservation according to caste and population is necessary in the Lok Sabha and Legislative Assembly to achieve political equality, and highlighted that the Backward Classes Department's budget had increased from Rs 5 crore when established to Rs 4,461 crore in 2026-27, with 2,712 hostels housing 2.58 lakh backward-community students.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Siddaramaiah demands 75% reservation for Dalits, BCs, minorities
- **Told:** Former Karnataka Chief Minister Siddaramaiah on Sunday called for implementation of the caste census and increase of reservation for Dalits, backward communities and minorities to 75 per cent at the Karnataka State Backward Castes Federation's Silver Jubilee celebration in Bengaluru.
- **Purpose:** Siddaramaiah said reservation according to caste and population is necessary in the Lok Sabha and Legislative Assembly to achieve political equality, and highlighted that the Backward Classes Department's budget had increased from Rs 5 crore when established to Rs 4,461 crore in 2026-27, with 2,712 hostels housing 2.58 lakh backward-community students.

**Important points**
- Siddaramaiah said reservation according to caste and population is necessary in the Lok Sabha and Legislative Assembly to achieve political equality, and highlighted that the Backward Classes Department's budget had increased from Rs 5 crore when established to Rs 4,461 crore in 2026-27, with 2,712 hostels housing 2.58 lakh backward-community students.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.1; News Trail Mangaluru p.5_

#### Police constable recruitment exam held amidst strict security

**In short:** The Karnataka Examinations Authority (KEA) conducted the written exam on Sunday for recruitment to Police Constable posts in the City and District Armed Reserve Force (CAR/DAR), with 1,14,079 of 1,42,220 candidates (80%) appearing; of 32,191 candidates from Kalyana Karnataka, 28,849 (90%) appeared, Executive Director H. Prasanna said.

The Karnataka Examinations Authority (KEA) conducted the written exam on Sunday for recruitment to Police Constable posts in the City and District Armed Reserve Force (CAR/DAR), with 1,14,079 of 1,42,220 candidates (80%) appearing; of 32,191 candidates from Kalyana Karnataka, 28,849 (90%) appeared, Executive Director H. Prasanna said. The exam was held without disturbance at 309 centres in the morning and 60 in the afternoon, with tight police presence and webcasting from a command centre at the KEA office in Bengaluru monitoring all centres. After the exam, OMR answer sheets reaching KEA are being scanned immediately and uploaded to the website in a transparent process open for public viewing.

**What this is**

- **Told:** The Karnataka Examinations Authority (KEA) conducted the written exam on Sunday for recruitment to Police Constable posts in the City and District Armed Reserve Force (CAR/DAR), with 1,14,079 of 1,42,220 candidates (80%) appearing; of 32,191 candidates from Kalyana Karnataka, 28,849 (90%) appeared, Executive Director H.
- **Purpose:** After the exam, OMR answer sheets reaching KEA are being scanned immediately and uploaded to the website in a transparent process open for public viewing.

#### Hockey club kitchen found unhygienic

**In short:** Food Safety and Drug Administration officials inspected Vivekanagar Hockey Club in Bengaluru on Saturday and found its kitchen maintained under unhygienic conditions, seizing expired and mislabelled food products. Commissioner Srinivas K said a notice was issued to the Food Business Operator and further legal action will follow under the Food Safety and Standards Act, 2006 and applicable Regulations, 2011.

Food Safety and Drug Administration officials inspected Vivekanagar Hockey Club in Bengaluru on Saturday and found its kitchen maintained under unhygienic conditions, seizing expired and mislabelled food products. Commissioner Srinivas K said a notice was issued to the Food Business Operator and further legal action will follow under the Food Safety and Standards Act, 2006 and applicable Regulations, 2011. Officials found 40 bottles of expired soft drinks, improperly labelled soda bottles not conforming to FSSAI requirements, unhygienic food preparation and storage areas, failure to follow required food safety measures, and pest infestation in preparation areas that may lead to contamination; the department said continuous inspections will continue.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Hockey club kitchen found unhygienic
- **Told:** Food Safety and Drug Administration officials inspected Vivekanagar Hockey Club in Bengaluru on Saturday and found its kitchen maintained under unhygienic conditions, seizing expired and mislabelled food products.
- **Purpose:** Officials found 40 bottles of expired soft drinks, improperly labelled soda bottles not conforming to FSSAI requirements, unhygienic food preparation and storage areas, failure to follow required food safety measures, and pest infestation in preparation areas that may lead to contamination; the department said continuous inspections will continue.

**Important points**
- Officials found 40 bottles of expired soft drinks, improperly labelled soda bottles not conforming to FSSAI requirements, unhygienic food preparation and storage areas, failure to follow required food safety measures, and pest infestation in preparation areas that may lead to contamination.
- the department said continuous inspections will continue.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.2_

#### Accident on flyover leaves driver injured

**In short:** A concrete mixer lorry crashed into a stationary container vehicle that had broken down on a flyover under Yelahanka Traffic Police Station jurisdiction last night, leaving one person seriously injured, police said. The container vehicle was stationary on the flyover when the mixer lorry coming from behind crashed into it, severely damaging the front portion of the mixer lorry.

#### Lawyer ordered to plant 1k trees for threatening judge

**In short:** The Karnataka High Court reportedly ordered advocate S Rangaswamy to plant and nurture 1,000 fruit trees after he threatened a trial judge during a court proceeding. The bench accepted his unconditional apology, dropping criminal contempt charges and imposing a small fine, but made clear that serious observations regarding his professional misconduct will remain officially recorded in the final order, stressing that protecting the dignity and safety of trial courts is vital while choosing leniency.

#### Karnataka govt to scrap NPS, reintroduce OPS soon: Dy CM

**In short:** Deputy Chief Minister G Parameshwara said on Saturday the Karnataka government will soon scrap the New Pension Scheme (NPS) and implement the Old Pension Scheme (OPS), calling reintroduction of OPS a long-standing demand of government employees and a Congress election promise. Addressing a state-level Teachers’ Day celebration organised by the School Education and Literacy and Higher Education departments, he said the chief minister has held discussions, the government has taken an important decision to scrap NPS and implement OPS soon, and Chief Minister D K Shivakumar was keen on scrapping NPS though OPS could not be reintroduced earlier for certain reasons.

#### Two police constables suspended for accepting bribes

**In short:** Kalaburagi City Police Commissioner Dr. Sharanappa S.D.

Kalaburagi City Police Commissioner Dr. Sharanappa S.D. ordered the suspension of Brahmapur Police Station constables Parashuram and Veeresh after they were caught by the Lokayukta while allegedly accepting a bribe from a rowdy-sheeter. On September 3, the constables allegedly threatened habitual offender Karthik of Bapu Nagar with externment if he did not pay a demanded bribe of ₹1 lakh, having already received ₹50,000 earlier; Parashuram was caught red-handed accepting ₹20,000 inside the police station premises while Veeresh fled, and departmental inquiries were initiated against both until further orders.

**Important points**
- ordered the suspension of Brahmapur Police Station constables Parashuram and Veeresh after they were caught by the Lokayukta while allegedly accepting a bribe from a rowdy-sheeter.
- On September 3, the constables allegedly threatened habitual offender Karthik of Bapu Nagar with externment if he did not pay a demanded bribe of ₹1 lakh, having already received ₹50,000 earlier.

#### CM: Students should become job creators, not stay job seekers

**In short:** Karnataka Chief Minister DK Shivakumar on Sunday said the state possesses talented human resources unmatched by any other state and stressed the need to provide students with opportunities to acquire global education and skills. Launching the 'Study Abroad 2026' programme at Palace Grounds in Bengaluru, he said the government wanted Karnataka students to learn at a global level and emerge as job creators, not merely employees; the programme brought together more than 80 universities offering study in over 300 disciplines, with scholarships from various departments and education loans of up to Rs 2 crore, and he said Karnataka had signed an agreement with Queensland University with 3,145 students selected for foreign studies.

Shivakumar highlighted Bengaluru's 2.6 million IT professionals and production of more than one lakh engineers and 13,940 doctors every year.

#### Three months in the making and gone within days: Dharwad students left struggling with poor-quality school shoes

**In short:** Government school students in Dharwad are facing torn shoes supplied under the state's free footwear scheme within a week of distribution, nearly three months after the academic year commenced, prompting parents to question quality under the government's new centralised procurement system. Earlier, schools purchased footwear locally through school development committees with funds allocated per student, but this year the government invited tenders at the state level and entrusted supply to selected contractors; parents want the education department to test samples and hold suppliers responsible if products failed quality specifications.

Earlier, schools purchased footwear locally through school development committees with funds allocated per student, but this year the government invited tenders at the state level and entrusted supply to selected contractors; parents want the education department to test samples and hold suppliers responsible if products failed quality specifications. School authorities said instructions had been given to replace damaged pairs, but parents demanded a broader inspection rather than case-by-case handling.

#### MLA GT Deve Gowda takes out rally, JD(S) leaders' pictures missing

**In short:** JD(S) MLA G T Deve Gowda on Sunday held a massive procession in Mysuru without posters or names of other party leaders, asserting the gathering from Chamundeshwari constituency demonstrated people's affection amid apparent differences with JD(S) second-in-command H D Kumaraswamy. Deve Gowda, recently made president of the state Cooperative Federation, said he was not invited to party programmes for three years and held the procession from Kote Anjaneya Swamy Temple to J K Grounds in response to that humiliation; the event was organised without photographs of other JD(S) leaders and saw him felicitated with garlands lifted using JCB machines.

#### Railway police conduct late-night check on B'luru-Chhattisgarh train in Davangere

**In short:** Railway Protection Force personnel and railway police conducted an intensive inspection of a Bengaluru-Chhattisgarh train at Davanagere railway station late Saturday night following information that suspected illegal Bangladeshi immigrants were travelling on the train. Police checked several coaches, verified identity cards and documents of more than 10 youths and a woman who had raised suspicion, with the alert reportedly shared by activists of the Hindu Jagarana Vedike; a similar check was conducted at Arsikere Junction.

Railway Protection Force personnel and railway police conducted an intensive inspection of a Bengaluru-Chhattisgarh train at Davanagere railway station late Saturday night following information that suspected illegal Bangladeshi immigrants were travelling on the train. Police checked several coaches, verified identity cards and documents of more than 10 youths and a woman who had raised suspicion, with the alert reportedly shared by activists of the Hindu Jagarana Vedike; a similar check was conducted at Arsikere Junction. Davanagere Railway Police inspector Krishna Naik clarified that no illegal Bangladeshi immigrants were found and police had only verified documents of seven passengers from Uttar Pradesh, calling the operation precautionary.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Railway police conduct late-night check on B'luru-Chhattisgarh train in Davangere

#### Melting glaciers, big dams and climate change make NE vulnerable to disasters

**In short:** Experts and activists warn that melting Eastern Himalayan glaciers, large hydropower projects in Tibet, Bhutan and India, and climate change are raising disaster risks in the Northeast, citing Nepal-like dangers after recent flash floods in Arunachal Pradesh, Assam's Sivasagar and Sikkim's 2023 GLOF devastation. Researchers including Mirza Zulfiqur Rahman and anti-dam activist Ebo Mili call for comprehensive ecological and vulnerability audits, regional cooperation and caution against aggressive dam building near glacial lakes.

Experts and activists warn that melting Eastern Himalayan glaciers, large hydropower projects in Tibet, Bhutan and India, and climate change are raising disaster risks in the Northeast, citing Nepal-like dangers after recent flash floods in Arunachal Pradesh, Assam's Sivasagar and Sikkim's 2023 GLOF devastation. Researchers including Mirza Zulfiqur Rahman and anti-dam activist Ebo Mili call for comprehensive ecological and vulnerability audits, regional cooperation and caution against aggressive dam building near glacial lakes.

- Assam's Sivasagar and Sikkim's 2023 GLOF devastation. Researchers including Mirza Zulfiqur Rahman and anti-dam activist Ebo Mili call for comprehensive ecological and vulnerability audits, regional cooperation and caution against aggressive dam building near glacial lakes.

#### 5 die as boys' PG collapses in Delhi

**In short:** An around 50-year-old five-storey building housing a boys' hostel in Satya Niketan near Delhi University's South Campus collapsed on Sunday afternoon, leaving five students dead and many trapped, with locals claiming 30-50 people may have been inside. Two people pulled from the debris were brought dead to hospital and three died during treatment; four were being treated at AIIMS Trauma Centre, while repair work was underway in the basement PG accommodation and two NDRF teams comprising 70 personnel and a canine squad were deployed after locals said rainwater collected in the basement had weakened the foundation.

An around 50-year-old five-storey building housing a boys' hostel in Satya Niketan near Delhi University's South Campus collapsed on Sunday afternoon, leaving five students dead and many trapped, with locals claiming 30-50 people may have been inside. Two people pulled from the debris were brought dead to hospital and three died during treatment; four were being treated at AIIMS Trauma Centre, while repair work was underway in the basement PG accommodation and two NDRF teams comprising 70 personnel and a canine squad were deployed after locals said rainwater collected in the basement had weakened the foundation.

#### Over 22 lakh affected by floods in Bihar

**In short:** More than 22 lakh people in 12 districts of Bihar have been affected by a deluge as rivers overflowed following heavy rain in the state and neighbouring Jharkhand, with the Ganga crossing its highest flood level in Patna on Sunday at 50.57 metres—0.05 metres above the previous HFL of 50.52 metres recorded on August 21. East Central Railway cancelled and diverted some trains because of alarmingly high water at a bridge connecting Punpun and Parsa Bazar stations; 190 community kitchens and 1,429 boats are in service, 27 SDRF and 10 NDRF teams are deployed, and around 22.42 lakh people in Patna, Bhojpur, Saran, Vaishali, Bhagalpur, Begusarai, Buxar, Samastipur, Munger, Katihar, Lakhisarai and Arwal are affected, with September rainfall so far at 69.1 mm, 55% above normal.

#### 'Every Indian aged over 18 yrs should get cholesterol levels checked'

**In short:** Renowned cardiac surgeon Dr Devi Prasad Shetty told DH that Indians should start monitoring heart health early, recommending everyone above 18 undergo a blood test for cholesterol and those above 40 consider a CT scan every few years to detect blockages, as Indians suffer heart attacks at much younger ages than European counterparts. He said treadmill stress tests identify blockages only above about 70%, warned that family history makes regular check-ups essential, praised step counters and arrhythmia-detecting wearables, and noted that around 75,000 medical seats added in the last 10 years plus expanding insurance and indigenous equipment are improving affordability of advanced cardiac care beyond major cities.

#### UN-backed map must reflect Indian position on J&K, Ladakh: govt.

**In short:** India said on Sunday that a United Nations-initiated proposed world map must depict the Union Territories of Jammu and Kashmir and Ladakh in accordance with India's official map, and any inaccurate or misleading representation is unacceptable, two days after it voted in favour of the UN General Assembly resolution "Correct the Map: Rebalancing Global Cartographic Representation and Promoting Equitable Representation of the World's Regions, Particularly Africa" adopted on Friday. External Affairs Ministry spokesperson Randhir Jaiswal said India highlighted that the resolution does not endorse any specific map, projection or depiction of national boundaries, and that India's position on sovereignty and territorial integrity is clear, consistent and non-negotiable.

#### SIR reunites long-lost brothers after 35 years in Haryana's Jind

**In short:** Sita Ram, 55, of Amritsar, who left his family in Jind 35 years ago to avoid burdening them with polio treatment costs, was reunited with his brother Dalbir, 60, after arriving at Government Senior Secondary School, Manoharpur, on Wednesday to collect his School Leaving Certificate for Special Intensive Revision verification. Booth Level Officer Krishan Kumar, who knew Dalbir, called him during the visit, leading to an emotional reunion at the school.

#### Soren govt loses 3 education-linked ministers since 2023

**In short:** JMM leader and Higher and Technical Education Minister Sudivya Kumar died of cardiac arrest early Sunday in Giridih, becoming the third minister linked to the education portfolio to die in Jharkhand since 2023 after Jagarnath Mahto (2023) and Ramdas Soren (2025), and the fourth serving minister in Hemant Soren's government since 2019 to pass away. Kumar, 56, was a close aide of the chief minister and had helped engage students during recent agitation over alleged job exam irregularities in Ranchi.

#### UNEARTHED PAST

**In short:** Workers uncovered a nearly 20-foot-deep, around 1.5-metre-wide well beneath debris of a demolished mosque in Saharanpur, Uttar Pradesh, on Sunday, a day after bulldozers razed the mosque in a nearly 16-hour operation on Saturday; officials said the well appeared about a century old and its exact age and historical significance would be established after examination by archaeological experts.

#### Multiplier in motor accident claims to be based on age of deceased: SC

**In short:** The Supreme Court held that the multiplier for motor-accident compensation must be determined solely by the deceased's age, not dependents' age, reaffirming Sarla Verma (2009) and Pranay Sethi (2017), in appeals from a 2011 Gurugram accident killing Ericsson manager Sushant Prabhakaran, 33. The court upheld a multiplier of 16 based on his age and awarded pillion rider Priyanka Das Rs 3,77,84,297 for 100% permanent physical impairment on a medical board finding, though it declined to recognise her as legally wedded wife for his death claim.

The Supreme Court held that the multiplier for motor-accident compensation must be determined solely by the deceased's age, not dependents' age, reaffirming Sarla Verma (2009) and Pranay Sethi (2017), in appeals from a 2011 Gurugram accident killing Ericsson manager Sushant Prabhakaran, 33. The court upheld a multiplier of 16 based on his age and awarded pillion rider Priyanka Das Rs 3,77,84,297 for 100% permanent physical impairment on a medical board finding, though it declined to recognise her as legally wedded wife for his death claim.

- **Purpose:** The court upheld a multiplier of 16 based on his age and awarded pillion rider Priyanka Das Rs 3,77,84,297 for 100% permanent physical impairment on a medical board finding, though it declined to recognise her as legally wedded wife for his death claim.

#### File response in 4 weeks: SC to Maharashtra on plea over delay in local body polls

**In short:** The Supreme Court granted Maharashtra four weeks to respond to a writ petition by former Kusumbe Gat Zilla Parishad member Sangram Govindrao Patil challenging non-conduct of local body elections for over 506 days after tenure expired on January 16, 2025, and reservation structures exceeding the 50% ceiling. The plea cited 73.21% reserved seats in Dhule Zilla Parishad and up to 85.71% in Panchayat Samitis; the matter is listed for October 16.

#### Young couple found dead in lake in Keralam

**In short:** Abhirami, 19, and Rajeev, 26, who had lived together despite family opposition, were found dead in Sasthamcotta Lake in Kollam district in an apparent suicide on Sunday after reaching the lake on Saturday evening. Police said they had been in a relationship for some time and families had earlier sought police intervention.

#### Remembering PR Brahmananda

**In short:** Economist P R Brahmananda, born in 1926 to freedom fighter and Kannada journalist P R Ramaiya and Bangalore deputy mayor P R Jayalakshamma, built a distinguished career at the University of Mumbai and became known for his Wage Goods Model as an alternative to the Mahalanobis framework for India's Second Five-Year Plan. The tribute notes his nearly 30 books, his role in the "Bombay School of Thought," his opposition to his nephew studying abroad, and tributes after his death in January 2003 from Paul Samuelson and Manmohan Singh on his 100th birth anniversary.

#### The great political migration before Uttar Pradesh elections

**In short:** With less than six months to go before the Uttar Pradesh Assembly polls, defectors and aspirants are repositioning themselves, opening with AIMIM spokesperson Asim Waqar joining the Congress after Owaisi's party split the non-BJP vote in Bihar. The piece examines how party-hopping has long been normal in UP, BJP's dilemma over Ayodhya MLA Ved Prakash Gupta after the Ram Temple seat loss, and how migration patterns shifted from a BJP-bound bulk in 2014–2017 toward the SP-led bloc ahead of 2024, when 38 of 80 UP Lok Sabha MPs had fought under more than one party symbol.

#### Rahul will lead INDIA bloc in 2029 polls, says Venugopal amid 'Vijay for PM' chant

**In short:** Senior Congress leader Rahul Gandhi will lead the INDIA bloc in the 2029 Lok Sabha elections, AICC general secretary K C Venugopal said on Sunday in Thrissur, denying that the TVK had proposed Tamil Nadu Chief Minister C Joseph Vijay as the alliance's prime ministerial candidate. Responding to reports projecting Vijay for the post, Venugopal said the TVK had made no such demand and that "Rahul Gandhi will be the face of the INDIA alliance," adding Gandhi's campaigns on issues concerning students, farmers and youth had received wide attention and that the INDIA alliance remained strong despite differences among constituents in some states.

#### Students forced to live in unsafe PGs: Oppn

**In short:** Opposition parties attacked the Centre and Delhi government after the Satya Niketan hostel collapse, with Congress president Mallikarjun Kharge calling it an "avoidable tragedy" that exposed unsafe, overcrowded paying-guest accommodation caused by inadequate college hostels. Rahul Gandhi appealed for full rescue efforts for trapped Delhi University students, Arvind Kejriwal termed the collapse "extremely tragic and alarming," and the AAP demanded large-scale measures from the Rekha Gupta government to ensure public safety.

#### CPM begins rectification drive to strengthen party

**In short:** After its electoral setback in Kerala, the CPM has launched a rectification campaign targeting parliamentary deviations, weak collective functioning and failure to adopt mass lines, with a 73-question questionnaire circulated among leaders. General Secretary M A Baby said the politburo discussed individual experiences on these counts at its Friday–Saturday meeting, while the Central Committee will evaluate the drive on October 9–11.

#### Union minister opens toll gates after seeing long queue in Thrissur

**In short:** Union minister Suresh Gopi on Sunday directed operators to open all gates at the Paliyekkara toll plaza on the Kochi-Salem National Highway in his Thrissur Lok Sabha constituency after seeing vehicles queued for more than 2 km on both sides. Police said Gopi got out of his vehicle, joined security personnel in opening the gates and cleared the backlog in about half an hour.

#### 'Shocked' by casteist poster, IIT Mandi orders inquiry

**In short:** IIT Mandi has ordered an inquiry into a Bhagavad Gita-themed poster displayed during Janmashtami celebrations on September 4 that said Shudras should "serve higher classes," with Director Laxmidhar Behera attributing it to students' interpretation and distancing the institute from its contents. Behera, who teaches a Gita course and is linked to ISKCON-related initiatives, said he was shocked, condemned caste discrimination and instituted a committee to ensure the campus is not used to violate social harmony.

#### Kanthapuram's remarks: Muslim scholars' forums unite against Keralam CM

**In short:** A row over a Muslim scholar's remarks is embarrassing Kerala's Congress-led UDF government as the Samastha Kerala Jem-Iyyathul Ulama-EK faction, close to the IUML, criticised Chief Minister V D Satheesan despite its historic split from the AP faction led by Kanthapuram A P Aboobacker Musliyar. An editorial in the EK mouthpiece Suprabhatham accused Satheesan of double standards for opposing Kanthapuram's remarks on women's public appearance while backing restrictions on women entering Sabarimala, even as both Sunni forums align on the religious issue.

#### 'Mere harassment' insufficient to prove abetment to suicide: SC

**In short:** The Supreme Court acquitted Sanjay Kumar in a long-pending dowry-related case, holding that ordinary marital discord or general harassment is not enough for abetment to suicide under Section 306 IPC without clear evidence of specific abetment under Section 107. Setting aside a 2016 Himachal Pradesh High Court conviction that had overturned his 2010 trial-court acquittal, the bench said prosecution witnesses spoke only in general terms and did not cite specific incidents of torture, harassment or dowry demand.

#### Govt sells 4k tonnes of buffer onions

**In short:** The government has sold about 4,000 tonnes of buffer onions across 17 cities in the first ten days of a subsidised sale aimed at curbing skyrocketing prices, Consumer Affairs Secretary Nidhi Khare said on Sunday. The Centre, through NCCF and Nafed, holds a buffer stock of 1.21 lakh tonnes of onion for 2026 and is transporting stored onions via dedicated rail rake "Kanda Express" or trucks, selling at a subsidised rate of Rs 35 per kg in select price-sensitive cities; Khare said distribution has brought some relief with prices falling slightly.

#### J&K's first international film festival from today aims to open up opportunities

**In short:** Kashmir is hosting its first international film festival from September 7–10 under the theme Rang-e-Cinema, organised by the J&K Department of Information and Public Relations to turn the region's cinematic legacy into a platform for filmmakers, local talent and film tourism. The four-day International Film Festival of Jammu and Kashmir will feature screenings, discussions, masterclasses and attractions including a floating cinema on Dal Lake, with participants including Ramesh Sippy, Vidhu Vinod Chopra and an international jury led by Santosh Sivan.

#### 10 dead after consuming spurious liquor in MP's Sagar

**In short:** Ten persons died and others fell ill after consuming spurious liquor in two villages of Banda tehsil in Madhya Pradesh's Sagar district on Sunday, though local BJP MLA Virendra Lodhi said 11 had died and around 25 were shifted from Banda Civil Hospital for advanced care. Chief Minister Mohan Yadav ordered a probe and strict action, the administration sealed seven liquor shops as a precaution, and senior officials including the collector and superintendent of police reached the affected area; the tragedy follows 13 deaths from spurious liquor in Gujarat's Bhavnagar last month.

#### Maruti bets on new Baleno to boost pole position in premium hatch space

**In short:** Maruti Suzuki India Limited rolled out a new Baleno edition over the weekend from an introductory price of Rs 6,09,900 ex-showroom Bengaluru, adding segment-first Level 2 ADAS and a refreshed NEXWave design to its premium hatchback that has sold more than 1.8 million units since 2015. Powered by the new Z12E petrol engine with up to 24.77 kmpl AGS efficiency and 33.61 km/kg S-CNG, the car also gets six airbags and expanded safety and driver-assistance features as MSIL averages 16,500 Baleno sales over the last five months.

#### IPO action intensifies as 11 cos target Rs 7,055 cr next week

**In short:** Eleven main-board companies including Rentomojo, Karamtara Engineering and Kanohar Electricals are set to raise Rs 7,055 crore through IPOs scheduled between September 7 and September 15, with most issues opening for subscription on Tuesday and Wednesday. Rajkumar Rathi, Chief Investment Officer at YES Securities, said the multi-issue rush reflects eagerness to lock in early-stage growth equity in young companies below USD 1 billion market cap at listing, spanning real estate, engineering, manufacturing, payments, specialty chemicals and an online rental platform.

#### GST Council meeting put off to October 7

**In short:** The GST Council meeting originally scheduled for September 12 has been postponed to October 7 because India is hosting the annual BRICS summit in New Delhi on September 12–13. The rescheduled 57th council meeting will be preceded by officers' meetings on October 5–6 and is likely to discuss simplification of GST registration for businesses passing tax credit of over Rs 2.5 lakh a month and automation in registration cancellation.

#### SBI to hire 12 k persons, add 250 branches

**In short:** State Bank of India intends to hire about 12,000 personnel and add around 250 new branches in the ongoing financial year, Chairman C S Setty said, with appointments across both clerical and officer cadres depending on retirements and emerging requirements. SBI, with a workforce of over 2.45 lakh employees as of March 2026, hired 4,640 officers, 19,340 associates and 1,653 contractual staff in 2025-26 totalling 5,633 hires; Setty noted last year's large recruitment included 1,500 specialist IT officers but fewer IT hires are needed this year.

#### Hyderabad's new mfg hotspot: Aero & defence outpace pharma

**In short:** Hyderabad's distance from international borders and location outside major seismic zones made it India's premier defence hub decades ago, but private and multinational capital has now transformed the city into a global aerospace and defence supply-chain node with Lockheed Martin, Boeing, Sikorsky and Safran among investors. Telangana aerospace and defence exports reached nearly Rs 32,850 crore in 2025-26—33.4% of state exports, overtaking pharma at roughly Rs 24,000 crore—and more than doubled from Rs 15,940 crore in 2023-24, with over 2,000 aircraft components shipped to the US and UK.

Hyderabad's distance from international borders and location outside major seismic zones made it India's premier defence hub decades ago, but private and multinational capital has now transformed the city into a global aerospace and defence supply-chain node with Lockheed Martin, Boeing, Sikorsky and Safran among investors. Telangana aerospace and defence exports reached nearly Rs 32,850 crore in 2025-26—33.4% of state exports, overtaking pharma at roughly Rs 24,000 crore—and more than doubled from Rs 15,940 crore in 2023-24, with over 2,000 aircraft components shipped to the US and UK. Major facilities include Safran's aero-engine plants, Kalyani Rafael's first private missile factory, Tata-Lockheed metal bonding at Adibatla, and Sigma Advance Systems' fresh £125 million Rolls-Royce deal through Bromford Precision Solutions.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Hyderabad's new mfg hotspot: Aero & defence outpace pharma
- **Told:** Hyderabad's distance from international borders and location outside major seismic zones made it India's premier defence hub decades ago, but private and multinational capital has now transformed the city into a global aerospace and defence supply-chain node with Lockheed Martin, Boeing, Sikorsky and Safran among investors.
- **Purpose:** Major facilities include Safran's aero-engine plants, Kalyani Rafael's first private missile factory, Tata-Lockheed metal bonding at Adibatla, and Sigma Advance Systems' fresh £125 million Rolls-Royce deal through Bromford Precision Solutions.

**Important points**
- Major facilities include Safran's aero-engine plants.
- Kalyani Rafael's first private missile factory.
- Tata-Lockheed metal bonding at Adibatla, and Sigma Advance Systems' fresh £125 million Rolls-Royce deal through Bromford Precision Solutions.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.13_

#### Equities likely to consolidate this week

**In short:** Indian equities are likely to consolidate after four consecutive weeks of decline, with elevated crude prices and global bond yields keeping sentiment cautious ahead of the US Fed policy decision on 15–16 September. Last week the Nifty 50 fell 1.1% to 23,898, Nifty Midcap 100 dropped 1.5%, and Nifty Smallcap 100 edged up 0.1%, with Oil & Gas the only major gainer (+1.1%) and Auto the biggest laggard (-4.0%).

Indian equities are likely to consolidate after four consecutive weeks of decline, with elevated crude prices and global bond yields keeping sentiment cautious ahead of the US Fed policy decision on 15–16 September. Last week the Nifty 50 fell 1.1% to 23,898, Nifty Midcap 100 dropped 1.5%, and Nifty Smallcap 100 edged up 0.1%, with Oil & Gas the only major gainer (+1.1%) and Auto the biggest laggard (-4.0%). Domestic fundamentals remain supportive, with GDP growing 7.8% YoY in 1QFY27, GST collections up 14.8% YoY to Rs 1.99 lakh crore in August, Japan Credit Rating Agency upgrading India's sovereign rating to A-, and record FCNR(B) inflows pushing system liquidity surplus above Rs 6 lakh crore.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Equities likely to consolidate this week
- **Told:** Indian equities are likely to consolidate after four consecutive weeks of decline, with elevated crude prices and global bond yields keeping sentiment cautious ahead of the US Fed policy decision on 15–16 September.

**Important points**
- Domestic fundamentals remain supportive, with GDP growing 7.8% YoY in 1QFY27.
- GST collections up 14.8% YoY to Rs 1.99 lakh crore in August.
- Japan Credit Rating Agency upgrading India's sovereign rating to A-, and record FCNR(B) inflows pushing system liquidity surplus above Rs 6 lakh crore.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.13_

#### DEATH-TRAP in capital: 3 dead... how YOUNG suffer

**In short:** An around 50-year-old five-storey building housing a boys' hostel in a congested bylane in Satya Niketan near Delhi University's South Campus collapsed on Sunday afternoon at around 1.30 pm, leaving three students dead and many trapped; eight people have been pulled from the debris, two brought dead and a third died during treatment, while five are critical. Locals claimed 40 to 50 people could still be trapped in the basement-and-five-floor structure known as 'Hostel Daze', whose owner is based in Gurugram, as two NDRF teams with 70 personnel and a canine squad searched the rubble and Union Home Minister Amit Shah spoke to the chief minister and NDMA officials; students paid around Rs 12,000 per bed, repair work was underway in the basement when it collapsed, and locals said water collected during days of rain may have weakened the foundation.

#### You can't write own report card: Sibal to PM

**In short:** Rajya Sabha MP Kapil Sibal on Sunday took a swipe at Prime Minister Modi over his speech at SRCC, saying he cannot write his own report card and that the people will do so. #### Prof Kemparaju: Urgent steps needed to improve quality of India’s higher education

**In short:** Former Bangalore North University Vice Chancellor Professor T.D. Kemparaju warned at a recent Teachers’ Day programme in Bengaluru that the government must take immediate steps to improve higher education quality, noting literacy has risen since Independence but higher-education enrolment growth is unsatisfactory despite more than 1,200 universities and 40,000 colleges and a Gross Enrolment Ratio of only 38%, with very few world-class institutions.

Former Bangalore North University Vice Chancellor Professor T.D. Kemparaju warned at a recent Teachers’ Day programme in Bengaluru that the government must take immediate steps to improve higher education quality, noting literacy has risen since Independence but higher-education enrolment growth is unsatisfactory despite more than 1,200 universities and 40,000 colleges and a Gross Enrolment Ratio of only 38%, with very few world-class institutions. He lamented declining enrolment in public universities and a shortage of permanent faculty hurting teaching and research, and urged Central and State governments to strengthen public universities facing competition from private and foreign institutions. Presiding over the function, Vice Chancellor Professor Ramesh B. called on teachers to carry forward the legacy of Central College, and Kemparaju urged lecturers to follow Dr. Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan’s example in guiding youth.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Prof Kemparaju: Urgent steps needed to improve quality of India’s higher education
- **Told:** Former Bangalore North University Vice Chancellor Professor T.D.
- **Purpose:** Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan’s example in guiding youth.

**Important points**
- He lamented declining enrolment in public universities and a shortage of permanent faculty hurting teaching and research, and urged Central and State governments to strengthen public universities facing competition from private and foreign institutions.
- Presiding over the function, Vice Chancellor Professor Ramesh B.
- called on teachers to carry forward the legacy of Central College, and Kemparaju urged lecturers to follow Dr.
- Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan’s example in guiding youth.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.2_

#### Sena 'govindas' turn hospital into battleground; 17 booked

**In short:** More than a dozen Shiv Sena functionaries, including Palghar district and city unit chiefs and an MLA's son, were booked on Sunday for allegedly assaulting, abusing and threatening hospital staff over medical bills of injured 'govindas' amid Dahi Handi celebrations. Of the 17 booked, 10 were named, including district chief Kundan Balkrishna Sankhe, city chief Rahul Ramdas Gharat and MLA Rajendra Gavit's son Rohit Gavit, after a mob led by Sankhe and Gharat entered Relief Hospital at 1:15 am, slapped receptionist Vivek Babulinga and barged into the ICU; five injured participants had a total bill of Rs 19,866, of which Rs 10,000 was paid in cash.

More than a dozen Shiv Sena functionaries, including Palghar district and city unit chiefs and an MLA's son, were booked on Sunday for allegedly assaulting, abusing and threatening hospital staff over medical bills of injured 'govindas' amid Dahi Handi celebrations. Of the 17 booked, 10 were named, including district chief Kundan Balkrishna Sankhe, city chief Rahul Ramdas Gharat and MLA Rajendra Gavit's son Rohit Gavit, after a mob led by Sankhe and Gharat entered Relief Hospital at 1:15 am, slapped receptionist Vivek Babulinga and barged into the ICU; five injured participants had a total bill of Rs 19,866, of which Rs 10,000 was paid in cash. A case was registered under the Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita and the Maharashtra Medicare Service Persons Act, and no arrests had been made as of Sunday.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Sena 'govindas' turn hospital into battleground; 17 booked

#### VHP's Garba 'diktat' unconstitutional, says NCP (SP)

**In short:** The NCP (SP) on Sunday slammed the Vishva Hindu Parishad's call to bar Muslims from participating in Garba events during Navratri, terming it "unconstitutional," and questioned the BJP-led Maharashtra government's silence. The VHP has announced that Muslims would not be allowed entry to 'Garba' and 'Dandiya' venues across the state during the upcoming Navratri festival beginning October 11, and issued guidelines for organisers to verify participants' identities by checking Aadhaar cards and apply 'tilak' on their foreheads.

NCP (SP) spokesperson Clyde Crasto said the BJP-led government should prove nobody is above the law by immediately taking action against the VHP's diktat.
