#### Telangana monsoon session set for stormy start today

**In short:** The monsoon session of the Telangana assembly beginning on Monday is expected to be stormy as the ruling Congress and main opposition BRS prepare to lock horns while the BJP looks to pressure the government on various issues. Chief Minister A Revanth Reddy accused BRS president K Chandrasekhar Rao of confining himself to his farmhouse and mostly staying away from the assembly since losing power in December 2023, vowing to expose the BRS and challenging KCR to debate the Congress government's first 1,000 days versus the previous BRS regime's 10-year rule.

The monsoon session of the Telangana assembly beginning on Monday is expected to be stormy as the ruling Congress and main opposition BRS prepare to lock horns while the BJP looks to pressure the government on various issues. Chief Minister A Revanth Reddy accused BRS president K Chandrasekhar Rao of confining himself to his farmhouse and mostly staying away from the assembly since losing power in December 2023, vowing to expose the BRS and challenging KCR to debate the Congress government's first 1,000 days versus the previous BRS regime's 10-year rule. BRS Working President K T Rama Rao released a 'chargesheet' alleging unfulfilled promises including tuition fees reimbursement dues, the Rs five lakh 'bharosa' card for students, 'scootys' for girl students and merger of polytechnic with Young India Skills University, while the BJP is preparing to attack the government on Section 22 A land registration issues and other unfulfilled promises.

#### Family of 4 jumps into Godavari; 3 dead, 1 rescued

**In short:** A family of four reportedly jumped into the Godavari River from a bridge at Elamanchili in Andhra Pradesh around 6.15 am on Saturday, police said. Search operations on Sunday led to the tracing of three bodies, while Namburi Padmini (42) was rescued by local fishermen and shifted to the Government Hospital in Razole; two motorcycles found at the scene helped identify the family as residents of Tanuku town.

A family of four reportedly jumped into the Godavari River from a bridge at Elamanchili in Andhra Pradesh around 6.15 am on Saturday, police said. Search operations on Sunday led to the tracing of three bodies, while Namburi Padmini (42) was rescued by local fishermen and shifted to the Government Hospital in Razole; two motorcycles found at the scene helped identify the family as residents of Tanuku town. The three deceased were identified as Padmini's husband Namburi Srinivas (45) and their two minor children, and further investigation was underway to ascertain the circumstances.

#### Order regulates food safety, not festivals: FDA Commissioner Tukaram Mundhe

**In short:** Maharashtra FDA Commissioner Tukaram Mundhe said the administration's recent directive on food safety is aimed at monitoring and preventing food adulteration and not imposing restrictions on festivities, clarifying that the order regulates food safety, not festivals. After months of crackdown on adulteration, FDA introduced a year-round "Festival Enforcement Calendar" to target food adulteration during festivals, establishing a standing framework covering gatherings across communities under a legal framework for Food Business Operators that has existed since 2011.

Maharashtra FDA Commissioner Tukaram Mundhe said the administration's recent directive on food safety is aimed at monitoring and preventing food adulteration and not imposing restrictions on festivities, clarifying that the order regulates food safety, not festivals. After months of crackdown on adulteration, FDA introduced a year-round "Festival Enforcement Calendar" to target food adulteration during festivals, establishing a standing framework covering gatherings across communities under a legal framework for Food Business Operators that has existed since 2011. Mundhe said the religion-neutral SOP does not prohibit bhandaras, prasad, community food distribution or religious festivals, and aims to prevent illness while letting festivals be celebrated with faith, joy and tradition.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Order regulates food safety, not festivals: FDA Commissioner Tukaram Mundhe
- **Told:** Maharashtra FDA Commissioner Tukaram Mundhe said the administration's recent directive on food safety is aimed at monitoring and preventing food adulteration and not imposing restrictions on festivities, clarifying that the order regulates food safety, not festivals.
- **Purpose:** Mundhe said the religion-neutral SOP does not prohibit bhandaras, prasad, community food distribution or religious festivals, and aims to prevent illness while letting festivals be celebrated with faith, joy and tradition.

**Important points**

#### Dahi Handi celebration: 1 dead, 376 'govindas' injured in Mumbai

**In short:** A minor boy died while 376 'govindas' were injured during Dahi Handi celebrations in Mumbai on Saturday, civic officials said on Sunday. A seven-year-old boy died after a brick column collapsed on him when a group of children were celebrating Dahi Handi on their own at Ekta Welfare Society at Chikuwadi in Malvani; of the injured, 183 were reported from hospitals in the island city, 126 in the western suburbs and 62 in the eastern suburbs, with 53 currently hospitalised, 37 under treatment and 286 discharged after treatment.

#### Revanth Reddy congratulates Mahesh Goud on two years as Telangana Congress chief

**In short:** Telangana Chief Minister A Revanth Reddy on Sunday congratulated state Congress president B Mahesh Kumar Goud on completing two years in the post, saying Goud rose from an ordinary worker to Pradesh Congress Committee president. #### Mumbai's GSB Seva Mandal gets Rs 703 crore insurance cover for Ganesh festival celebration

**In short:** The GSB Seva Mandal in Mumbai has taken an insurance cover of Rs 703.27 crore for the upcoming Ganpati festival celebration, compared to Rs 474 crore last year, organisers said on Sunday. The deity's idol will be adorned with more than 66 kg of gold and 335 kg of silver ornaments during its five-day festival celebration from September 14 to 18, with the 10-day Ganpati festival beginning September 14 and the first look of the idol to be unveiled during the 'Virat Darshan' ceremony at 8 pm on September 12.

The GSB Seva Mandal in Mumbai has taken an insurance cover of Rs 703.27 crore for the upcoming Ganpati festival celebration, compared to Rs 474 crore last year, organisers said on Sunday. #### Viral Vietnam video falsely linked to Nepal floods

**In short:** A video from Vietnam has been falsely portrayed by several Indian media outlets as showing two young siblings searching for their parents after being separated during the devastating Nepal floods that have killed more than 1,280 people and left over 5,600 missing on both sides of the China-Nepal border. Outlets including The Times of India, India Today NE, Republic, Times Now and News18 shared the video with the Nepal-flood claim, but a reverse-image search by Boom found the original footage posted on Instagram by Vietnamese content creator @thienduyennet, and The Statesman later confirmed with the creator that it was filmed in Ha Giang, Vietnam.

#### Against odds, Keralam's Bushra finds way back to learning

**In short:** For 22-year-old Bushra of Kottakkal in Malappuram district, formal schooling ended in LKG because of family hardship, but she has returned through the Literacy Mission's non-formal programmes, clearing Class 4 and Class 7 equivalency exams and now preparing for Class 10 at Kottappadi Government Boys Higher Secondary School. The youngest equivalency learner enrolled with the district Literacy Mission, she was honoured at a function where District Coordinator P V Sastha Prasad draped a shawl around her, with support from her mother Sulaikha after her father A Shihabuddeen's death.

#### Caste census: Stalin urges Centre to be accurate

**In short:** DMK president M K Stalin expressed disappointment over the Union government's proposed open-ended format for recording caste details in Census 2027, calling on the Tamil Nadu government to pass a special Assembly resolution to press the Centre for an error-free enumeration. He alleged that allowing manual caste entries without a standardised drop-down list would undermine the census purpose, urged integration of state BC and MBC lists, and warned that proceeding with the current methodology would waste nearly Rs 12,000 crore of public funds.

#### 'Can shattered dreams be restored?': Rahul

**In short:** Congress leader Rahul Gandhi lashed out at Prime Minister Narendra Modi and Bihar Chief Minister Samrat Choudhary over police action against protesting students, sharing a video of his meeting with youngsters who endured "horrific police brutality" while raising voices against alleged examination irregularities. He said students from poor families seeking education, employment and accountability were shot, including one whose leg bone was broken by an AK-47 bullet, ending his dream of joining the army, and two others whose shoulders were torn by bullets.

#### Gopi calls for steps to prevent accidents

**In short:** Union Minister of State for Petroleum and Natural Gas Suresh Gopi called for stringent steps to prevent accidents like the one at Kaipamangalam in Thrissur that killed eight men from Tamil Nadu when their car hit a parked lorry on Friday night. The Thrissur MP said all stakeholders had made mistakes, the NHAI had been "strictly warned," and proper diversions and safety parameters were needed on National Highway stretches where construction was still underway rather than public blame-shifting.

#### Pilot in saddle as Cong looks to avoid Punjab crash-landing

**In short:** Congress appointed Sachin Pilot as general secretary in-charge of poll-bound Punjab on Saturday, replacing Bhupesh Baghel, entrusting the party's youth poster boy with addressing factional infighting ahead of early-next-year assembly elections. Baghel, a former Chhattisgarh chief minister, moves to Assam in the reshuffle, while Pilot's immediate challenge is to unify the state unit as rival parties step up preparations and the Congress has largely been firefighting internal feuds.

#### Jantar Mantar student stir, mass struggles could impact Assembly polls: MA Baby

**In short:** CPI(M) general secretary M A Baby said the Jantar Mantar student movement and other mass struggles could have a "very significant impact" on coming Assembly elections, asserting the protests helped break youth fear of the Narendra Modi government, the RSS and its leadership. He said the struggle's impact was already felt in the BJP's defeat in Bihar's Bankipur by-election, drew parallels with the Samyukt Kisan Morcha farmers' agitation before the Lok Sabha polls, and said the party would use its influence to defeat the BJP and allies while discussing seat-sharing state by state.

#### A river-cleaning effort wins him a Dutch job offer

**In short:** Surendra Singh Chaudhary, a 21-year-old student from Biaora in Madhya Pradesh's Rajgarh district known online as Bittu Tabahi, went viral for cleaning the heavily polluted Ajnar River clogged with plastic, algae and sludge, launching his campaign on January 26, 2026, and spending his own savings despite health risks. Boyan Slat, founder and CEO of Netherlands-based The Ocean Cleanup, saw his work on X and invited him with "Come work for us"; Madhya Pradesh Chief Minister Mohan Yadav also met him on September 4 and praised his initiative while assuring government support.

#### Hyundai Motor expects 30% rural sales: MD & CEO

**In short:** Hyundai Motor India Ltd expects rural markets to contribute around 30 per cent of total sales in the next three to four years, Managing Director and CEO Tarun Garg said, with rural contribution reaching 25.9 per cent in Q1 this fiscal year. The company, which gets 68 per cent of total sales from SUVs including 69 per cent SUV penetration in rural markets, is opening six out of every ten new outlets in rural areas and is betting on two upcoming products—a midsize SUV and a compact electric SUV—to drive further rural growth.

#### Govt moots FDI liberalisation in plantation

**In short:** The government is considering liberalising foreign direct investment norms in the plantation sector by bringing more commercial crops under the ambit, a Commerce and Industry Ministry official said during stakeholder consultations. At present 100 per cent FDI under the automatic route is permitted in tea, coffee, rubber, cardamom, palm and olive oil tree plantations but not in other plantation activities; India has received USD 295.23 million FDI in tea and coffee and USD 3.93 billion in rubber goods during April 2000 and March 2026.

#### Unicorn NoBroker's FY26 revenue crosses Rs 1k cr

**In short:** Bengaluru proptech startup NoBroker.com's revenue crossed Rs 1,000 crore in the last fiscal year and it aims to become profitable in the next 8-10 months, co-founder and CEO Amit Kumar Agarwal said. Revenue grew to Rs 965 crore in 2024-25 from Rs 888 crore in the preceding year, helping reduce losses by 25-30 per cent to around Rs 300 crore; the platform, established in 2013, lists properties for rent, buy and sell without brokerage fees and offers movers, packers and society management through NoBrokerHood.

#### Chalet Hotels targets 5,500 keys by FY30

**In short:** Chalet Hotels Ltd aims to have around 5,500 hotel keys by FY30 as it expands beyond its traditional asset-ownership model, MD and CEO Shwetank Singh said, pursuing a combination of third-party operated hotels, franchise properties and hotels under its own Athiva brand accounting for 1,200-1,300 keys of the pipeline. Upcoming portfolio includes a 380-room Taj hotel at Delhi Airport with approximately 70 rooms expected by end of this financial year, Ritz-Carlton Hyderabad, Hyatt Regency Airoli and Udaipur hotel in FY29, and a Pune Yerawada project targeted for FY31.

#### India's festive season promises strong show as quick commerce reshapes online shopping battle

**In short:** India's e-commerce and quick commerce platforms are gearing up for the festive season with industry analysts expecting healthy value growth as premium demand coexists with bargain-hunting by value-conscious shoppers. An Infisum report projected India's e-commerce market to nearly triple from USD 125 billion in 2024 to USD 345 billion by 2030 at 18.4 per cent CAGR, accounting for 10-12 per cent of retail spending and serving 420-440 million online shoppers by decade's end; Zepto CBO Devendra Meel said brands are introducing festive-specific SKUs for occasions including Ekadashi and Teej.

#### FPIs resume selling; pull out Rs 7,443 cr

**In short:** Foreign portfolio investors turned net sellers in the first week of September, pulling out Rs 7,443 crore from Indian equities after infusing Rs 29,600 crore in August and Rs 20,200 crore in July, as rebounding crude oil prices, rising US bond yields and a firm dollar dented risk appetite. Total FPI outflow from Indian equities has climbed to Rs 2.32 lakh crore so far in 2026, surpassing Rs 1.66 lakh crore withdrawn in 2025; foreign investors also withdrew Rs 377 crore through FAR and Rs 231 crore through VRR from debt markets.

#### HUL aims 22-24% EBITDA margin in medium term

**In short:** Hindustan Unilever is targeting a 22-24 per cent EBITDA margin in the medium term and stepping up capital expenditure to 3 per cent of turnover from 2 per cent maintained over the last five years to chase growth. HUL, part of Unilever Plc, registered revenue of Rs 63,763 crore in FY26 with 21 brands each exceeding Rs 1,000 crore turnover, an EBITDA margin of 23.6 per cent down about 70 basis points year-on-year; MD and CEO Priya Nair said the company will unlock an incremental 500 basis points through a richer premium mix.

- **Purpose:** HUL, part of Unilever Plc, registered revenue of Rs 63,763 crore in FY26 with 21 brands each exceeding Rs 1,000 crore turnover, an EBITDA margin of 23.6 per cent down about 70 basis points year-on-year; MD and CEO Priya Nair said the company will unlock an incremental 500 basis points through a richer premium mix.

#### Novartis India to pursue therapy-aligned acquisitions

**In short:** Novartis India Ltd plans to pursue disciplined, therapy-aligned acquisitions and deepen last-mile reach into Tier 2 and Tier 3 markets, according to its annual report, focusing on five anchor therapy areas: pain management, wellness, women's health, neuroscience and transplant immunology. #### NMDC to start commercial coal production next quarter: Chairman

**In short:** State-owned NMDC will start commercial production of thermal coal in the October-December period and looks to sell around 1 MT within FY27, Chairman Amitava Mukherjee said; the Hyderabad-based company caters to 20 per cent of India's iron ore needs. NMDC has reached the coal seam of the Tokisud North coal mine with 52 MT reserves and 2.3 MT annual peak rated capacity won in auction, and will also start developing the Rohne coking coal block with 191 MT reserves within FY27 to begin production as early as FY28.

#### Snake boats to be standardised

**In short:** A Government-sponsored Committee prepared a scheme to standardise Kerala snake boats for Nehru Trophy races, as disparities in length and rower capacity—some boats carrying 70–80 rowers and others over 100—were letting larger boats dominate; the Committee is also considering ₹10,000 loans per boat for standardisation, with four major boat races scheduled in Central Travancore that weekend in September 1976.

#### No provision in Forest Rights Act to obtain gram sabha consent for projects: Ministry

**In short:** The Tribal Affairs Ministry told the Power Ministry on August 31 that the 2006 FRA has "no provision for obtaining consent of the Gram Sabha for forest clearance" and such matters do not fall under its purview, though Forest Conservation Act guidelines require completing FRA processes and gram sabha NOCs before diverting forest land; the exchange followed a Parliamentary panel report citing 106-month average forest clearance delays for NHPC projects such as Teesta-IV HEP.

#### 1.85 lakh names out of Manipur, Sikkim poll rolls after SIR

**In short:** Final photo electoral rolls published Sunday removed 1,32,456 names in Manipur (6.3% of pre-SIR 20,93,076 voters) and 52,614 in Sikkim (11.2% of 4,71,081), leaving Manipur with 19,60,620 electors and Sikkim with 4,18,467; Manipur now has 10,06,962 women voters versus 9,53,350 men, and Kuki-Zo groups had feared displaced community members would be deleted after the May 2023 ethnic conflict.

#### Punjab Police arrest man wanted in U.S. over fraud

**In short:** Punjab Police arrested naturalised American citizen Manjeet Singh Bedi in Jalandhar two days ago; officials said he allegedly defrauded the U.S. government of at least $600,000, carried a bounty of $1,15,000, left the United States in April and reached India via Canada to evade the law.

#### Road map ready to make India drug-free by 2029, says Shah

**In short:** Union Home Minister Amit Shah said in Panaji on Sunday that the government has prepared a road map to make India drug-free by 2029, asserting a drug-free youth population would lead to Viksit Bharat by 2047, after chairing a high-level narcotics meeting with Goa Chief Minister Pramod Sawant. He said the Centre's coordinated nationwide strategy targets the entire narcotics network from trafficking to financing while focusing on rehabilitation, through the four-tier NCORD mechanism created in 2019, dedicated Anti-Narcotics Task Forces in all States and Union Territories, a 2019 Joint Coordination Committee, a strengthened NCB, and the 1933 MANAS helpline for persons with drug addiction.

#### As yoga and Ayurveda become part of India's global health diplomacy, experts press for credible standards

**In short:** Yoga and Ayurveda are increasingly part of India's global health and diplomatic strategy, with trade agreements including the India-Oman CEPA operationalised in June 2026, the India-New Zealand FTA and the India-EU FTA signed in January 2026 easing licensing, standards and AYUSH practitioner mobility. Ministry of AYUSH officials said foreign medical arrivals rose from 1.83 lakh in 2020 to 6.44 lakh in 2024—though that figure covers overall foreign medical arrivals, not AYUSH patients specifically—and that 3,375 foreign nationals travelled on AYUSH or e-AYUSH visas between January 2023 and December 2025; experts warn credible standards, evidence, regulation and practitioner quality are needed for success in regulated global healthcare markets.

#### All the hard work is paying off, says Rudrankksh as he gears up for Asiad

**In short:** Rudrankksh Balasaheb Patil, 22, heading to his second Asian Games in Nagoya from September 14 after a preparatory camp in Bhopal, will compete in 10m air rifle and 50m 3 Position and said all his hard work is paying off. The only Indian male shooter in two events said his aim is gold, that he missed a 10m medal by the barest margins last time, and that he is now more in control of body and mind between events.

#### The graceful artist's brush with history

**In short:** Smriti Mandhana's 64-ball 124 against Hong Kong China at Dubai last Thursday made her the leading run-scorer in international women's cricket ahead of Mithali Raj and gave her 18 international hundreds, surpassing Meg Lanning and Laura Wolvaardt. P.K.

Smriti Mandhana's 64-ball 124 against Hong Kong China at Dubai last Thursday made her the leading run-scorer in international women's cricket ahead of Mithali Raj and gave her 18 international hundreds, surpassing Meg Lanning and Laura Wolvaardt. P.K. Ajith Kumar writes that the stylish opener, compared by peers to David Gower and Sourav Ganguly, has turned batting into an art form through years of hard work since her Sangli childhood and India debut in 2013.

- **Concept:** graceful artist's brush with history

#### Goal speed of Indian Railways’ hydrogen train

**In short:** Railway Board Chairman and CEO Satish Kumar said Indian Railways is working to operate the country’s first hydrogen-powered train at speeds of up to 110 kmph after completing trials at 120 kmph, with consultations under way among stakeholders to decide which rail sections will run the new train.

#### Percentage of voters deleted in Sikkim in SIR exercise

**In short:** Sikkim’s Chief Electoral Officer said 52,551 electors were deleted during the Special Intensive Revision, cutting the roll by about 11.16% from 4,71,018 to 4,18,467 eligible voters.

### International

#### Netanyahu orders West Bank settler outposts to be removed

**In short:** Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu has ordered unauthorized settler outposts in the West Bank to be dismantled, two people familiar with the matter said on Sunday, amid US pressure to crack down on settler attacks on Palestinians. The order applies to outposts established by settlers without official Israeli authorisation, typically prefabricated caravans housing a small number of settlers; it was not immediately clear when the operation would begin.

Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu has ordered unauthorized settler outposts in the West Bank to be dismantled, two people familiar with the matter said on Sunday, amid US pressure to crack down on settler attacks on Palestinians. The order applies to outposts established by settlers without official Israeli authorisation, typically prefabricated caravans housing a small number of settlers; it was not immediately clear when the operation would begin. U.N. bodies and most governments deem all Israeli settlements illegal under international law and consider the West Bank occupied territory, though Israel argues the territory is disputed.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Netanyahu orders West Bank settler outposts to be removed
- **Told:** Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu has ordered unauthorized settler outposts in the West Bank to be dismantled, two people familiar with the matter said on Sunday, amid US pressure to crack down on settler attacks on Palestinians.
- **Purpose:** bodies and most governments deem all Israeli settlements illegal under international law and consider the West Bank occupied territory, though Israel argues the territory is disputed.

**Important points**
- U.N.
- bodies and most governments deem all Israeli settlements illegal under international law and consider the West Bank occupied territory, though Israel argues the territory is disputed.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.14_

#### 4 dead in Afghan mine mishap

**In short:** Four men died of gas asphyxiation in a coal mine in northeastern Afghanistan's Tala Wa Barfak district, Baghlan province's information department reported on Sunday. Department head Sayed Mustafa Hashimi said the mine did not meet safety standards and the men suffocated while extracting coal.

Four men died of gas asphyxiation in a coal mine in northeastern Afghanistan's Tala Wa Barfak district, Baghlan province's information department reported on Sunday. Department head Sayed Mustafa Hashimi said the mine did not meet safety standards and the men suffocated while extracting coal. Afghanistan's poorly regulated mining industry sees frequent fatal accidents in coal, marble, mineral, gold and precious-stone operations.

#### Over 140 killed in Yemen clashes

**In short:** Fighting between Yemeni government forces and Iran-backed Houthi rebels in southwest Yemen killed more than 140 combatants between midday Saturday and early Sunday, military sources said. Two Saudi-backed government officials told AFP that 84 of their troops were killed, mostly in missile attacks, while four Houthi military sources said 57 rebel fighters were killed in the same period.

#### Jakarta airport suspends ops

**In short:** About 170,000 passengers were stranded on Sunday as Indonesia shuttered Soekarno-Hatta International Airport and seven others, cancelling hundreds of flights after Mount Anak Krakatoa spewed volcanic ash and lava from more than 150 kilometres away. The volcano erupted on Saturday with lava fountains and booming sounds heard hundreds of kilometres away, and two new eruptions were recorded on Sunday; ash in surrounding airspace prompted authorities to suspend operations from 1:30 am to 5:30 am Sunday.

#### Iran threatens greater force if US launches more attacks

**In short:** Iran's parliament speaker Mohammad Bagher Ghalibaf, acting as chief negotiator, threatened a faster, more intense and more painful response to any further US attacks after Tehran targeted American warships in the latest round of clashes amid a six-month war deadlock. US Central Command said Iran unsuccessfully fired at an aircraft carrier and a destroyer on Saturday, while the IRGC claimed the warships retreated after suffering damage; CENTCOM said it then struck three oil tankers linked to the Iranian Guards—M/T Downy off Kharg Island, M/T Stark 1 near Jask, and M/T Kylo in the Gulf of Oman.

Iran's parliament speaker Mohammad Bagher Ghalibaf, acting as chief negotiator, threatened a faster, more intense and more painful response to any further US attacks after Tehran targeted American warships in the latest round of clashes amid a six-month war deadlock. US Central Command said Iran unsuccessfully fired at an aircraft carrier and a destroyer on Saturday, while the IRGC claimed the warships retreated after suffering damage; CENTCOM said it then struck three oil tankers linked to the Iranian Guards—M/T Downy off Kharg Island, M/T Stark 1 near Jask, and M/T Kylo in the Gulf of Oman. Iran has maintained its stranglehold on the Strait of Hormuz while Washington continues a counter-blockade of Iranian ports.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Iran threatens greater force if US launches more attacks
- **Told:** Iran's parliament speaker Mohammad Bagher Ghalibaf, acting as chief negotiator, threatened a faster, more intense and more painful response to any further US attacks after Tehran targeted American warships in the latest round of clashes amid a six-month war deadlock.
- **Purpose:** Iran has maintained its stranglehold on the Strait of Hormuz while Washington continues a counter-blockade of Iranian ports.

**Important points**

_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.14_

#### B'desh: Hindu rights leader gets bail; release unlikely

**In short:** Bangladesh's High Court on Sunday granted bail to jailed Hindu rights leader Chinmoy Krishna Das in two more vandalism and obstruction cases, but his release remains unlikely as he faces charges in other cases. Das was arrested in November 2024 on sedition charges for allegedly disrespecting the national flag and was subsequently booked in several additional cases including murder, attempted murder and vandalism; he now has High Court bail in four of six criminal cases but will not be released immediately because of the remaining charges.

#### Iran to tackle economic issues amid rising tensions

**In short:** Iran said it will step up efforts to tackle problems created by US sanctions crippling its economy, while a senior official warned of a "painful response" if it comes under further attack, six months after US-Israeli strikes and with a preliminary June ceasefire accord unravelled. After tit-for-tat strikes resumed in September, US Central Command said it hit three Iranian tankers on Saturday following IRGC ballistic missile attacks on two US Navy ships; Parliament Speaker Mohammad Baqer Qalibaf said on Sunday that rules of the game in the war against Iran have changed and any attack on Iran's interests will receive a faster, heavier and more painful response.

#### Homes lost, hungry babies: Nepal's mothers struggle in shelters

**In short:** Nepal's August 26 flash floods separated Sharmila Shrestha from her 14-month-old daughter for two or three days and destroyed her home; reunited at a crowded Kathmandu shelter, she can only feed her toddler the same heavy adult food as other evacuees because no baby food is available. The UNFPA estimates around 4,430 pregnant women are living across 15 municipalities in three flood-affected districts based on an affected population of about 310,000, with roughly 492 births expected in the following month and about 74 women potentially requiring emergency obstetric care.

Nepal's August 26 flash floods separated Sharmila Shrestha from her 14-month-old daughter for two or three days and destroyed her home; reunited at a crowded Kathmandu shelter, she can only feed her toddler the same heavy adult food as other evacuees because no baby food is available. The UNFPA estimates around 4,430 pregnant women are living across 15 municipalities in three flood-affected districts based on an affected population of about 310,000, with roughly 492 births expected in the following month and about 74 women potentially requiring emergency obstetric care. Mothers report struggling to breastfeed, sleeping on classroom floors, and worrying about reaching hospitals if complications arise.

**What this is**

#### Nepal urges countries to limit travel advisories

**In short:** Nepal has urged friendly countries to confine travel advisories to specific flood-affected areas, highlighting that the rest of the country remains open and safe for travel and tourism. The Ministry of Foreign Affairs said on Sunday that it and Nepal's missions abroad were working closely with friendly countries on the issue, after the United States issued a September 4 advisory placing Nepal under Level 2 "Exercise increased caution" due to natural disasters.

#### Issues media safety protocol

**In short:** Nepal has issued a media safety and operational protocol for journalists visiting areas affected by the August 26 floods, and foreign journalists seeking to report from flood-affected areas must obtain press accreditation. The National Disaster Risk Reduction and Management Authority on Sunday posted the protocol issued by Nepal's Ministry of Information and Communication for journalists visiting flood-affected areas.

#### UN names Nov 27 as day against child marriage

**In short:** The 193-member UN General Assembly on Friday adopted by consensus a resolution proclaiming November 27 every year as the International Day for the Elimination of Child, Early and Forced Marriage, led by Sierra Leone with India among co-sponsors. The date marks the November 27, 2024 launch of India's 'Bal Vivah Mukt Bharat' campaign aiming to reduce child marriage prevalence by 10% by 2026 and make India child-marriage free by 2030, an initiative also championed by Sierra Leone's First Lady Fatima Maada Bio and Just Rights for Children Founder Bhuwan Ribhu.

#### Moscow not ruling out trilateral Russia, US, China meeting

**In short:** Russia's Deputy Foreign Minister Sergei Ryabkov said on Sunday that Moscow has not ruled out a trilateral meeting between the presidents of Russia, the US and China, but that the main question is how the meeting's substance and expected results would be formulated. Kremlin aide Yuri Ushakov had earlier raised the possibility of Vladimir Putin, Donald Trump and Xi Jinping meeting at the APEC summit in Shenzhen, China, in November; Ushakov said on Sunday such a meeting would not be without merit but its organisation is the prerogative of the summit hosts.

**In short:** US envoy Steve Witkoff said both Russia and Ukraine need to make "compromises" to end more than four years of war after holding talks with Ukraine's Volodymyr Zelensky, adding that while it is very difficult, the US is committed and hopes the diplomacy leads to a successful resolution.

#### Germany 'wants war again': Russia on drone accusations

**In short:** Russian Foreign Minister Sergei Lavrov said Germany "wants war again" in an interview published Sunday, dismissing Berlin's accusation that Russia sent low-level agents for an attempted hybrid attack involving an explosive-laden drone at Leipzig airport. Moscow and Berlin closed each other's cultural centres this week, with Lavrov accusing Germany of Nazism and saying the Russian leadership will draw appropriate conclusions; Germany, a main Ukraine ally, blamed Moscow for the incident at the strategic eastern airport.

#### Lebanon president calls for US help as Israeli strikes kill 7

**In short:** Lebanese President Joseph Aoun urged Washington and the international community to help stop Israeli attacks that killed at least seven people on Sunday despite a truce and framework deal aimed at halting violence. Israel said it targeted Hezbollah operatives and sites in response to a drone attack on its troops near a strategic south Lebanon ridge; Aoun called Sunday's strikes, which damaged government premises and destroyed an out-of-service hospital, a dangerous escalation reaching Lebanese state institutions and health facilities.

#### Trump admin makes another appeal over mail ballot curbs

**In short:** The Trump administration on Sunday renewed its appeal to the Supreme Court after US District Court Judge Indira Talwani extended an order blocking the Postal Service from enforcing President Donald Trump's executive order restricting mail ballots ahead of the midterms. Solicitor General John Sauer said ballots have already begun mailing in North Carolina and more states will follow while the injunction remains, marking the third time the administration has asked the high court to intervene.

#### Prince Harry's surprise UK return renews security row

**In short:** Prince Harry's return to Britain with wife Meghan and their two young children has reignited debate over whether taxpayers should fund their protection, with experts warning genuine threats likely exist. Harry, who quit royal duties in 2020 and lost a legal battle to retain UK taxpayer-funded security while living in California, has reportedly been pushing for reinstated police protection; when the family landed at Birmingham Airport on August 26 they had private security rather than police officers, according to British media.

#### FIFA confirms Infantino will run in presidential election

**In short:** Gianni Infantino will stand in March's FIFA presidential election despite backlash over his now-abandoned plan to bring private investment into the World Cup, the global governing body confirmed on Sunday. A FIFA spokesperson said the president announced in April that he will stand for re-election in 2027; Infantino had declined to answer questions about his future as he arrived for the opening of the women's U-20 World Cup in Poland.

#### Iranian school struck in war's earliest attacks is now memorial for children

**In short:** An Associated Press report from Minab describes the Shajareh Tayyebeh school—struck in the earliest moments of the war the United States and Israel began across Iran on February 28—as an emerging memorial to schoolchildren killed there, with Iranian officials saying more than 100 students died and AP finding evidence that at least one US missile hit the school while the Trump administration has not directly accepted responsibility or released Pentagon findings. The badly damaged two-storey building's classrooms remain frozen mid-lesson, Hormozgan Gov.

An Associated Press report from Minab describes the Shajareh Tayyebeh school—struck in the earliest moments of the war the United States and Israel began across Iran on February 28—as an emerging memorial to schoolchildren killed there, with Iranian officials saying more than 100 students died and AP finding evidence that at least one US missile hit the school while the Trump administration has not directly accepted responsibility or released Pentagon findings. The badly damaged two-storey building's classrooms remain frozen mid-lesson, Hormozgan Gov. Mohammad Ashouri Taziani has said it would become a war museum and memorial, and residents said three bodies still have not been found as attention returned to Minab after new US strikes along Iran's southern coast hit a wedding and other areas.

#### Indonesia cancels flights as volcano erupts

**In short:** Indonesia's Anak Krakatau volcano erupted early Sunday for 30 seconds at 3:53 a.m., prompting cancellation of all flights at Jakarta's Soekarno-Hatta International Airport as volcanic ash drifted across western Indonesia. Indonesia's Geological Agency said the Sunda Strait island had shown activity since Friday; no casualties or evacuation order was issued, though residents and visitors were warned to stay at least 3 kilometres from the active crater, with the nearest settlement more than 16 kilometres away.

#### Putin, US envoys revive peace talks

**In short:** Russian President Vladimir Putin met US envoys Steve Witkoff and Jared Kushner at the Kremlin on Saturday for more than three hours of closed-door talks to revive a stalled push to end the Russia-Ukraine war, with both sides agreeing to pause strikes on each other's capitals during the discussions. Putin ordered no strikes against Kyiv for three days from midnight, and Zelenskyy said Ukraine was prepared to halt strikes on Moscow through Monday; Washington's peace push has lost momentum as US attention has focused for six months on the Iran war, and the envoys were also due to visit Kyiv on Sunday.

#### Wedding party fire in Congo leaves 22 dead

**In short:** A fire at a wedding party in Congo's capital Kinshasa left at least 22 people dead and several others injured, Lingwala regional mayor Norbert Mushunga said Saturday. The fire started Friday night and continued into early Saturday in a building in northern Kinshasa's Lingwala area, where narrow hallways were constricted by people trying to escape; several people, alive and dead, were laid by the roadside as attendees tried to help survivors.

#### Iran strikes US ship in Strait of Hormuz

**In short:** Iran said Sunday it struck an unmanned US vessel trying to enter the Strait of Hormuz, the latest salvo in a flare-up of fighting that began with US and Israeli attacks on Feb 28 and has persisted despite a June ceasefire announcement. The US did not immediately confirm the attack, which came a day after US forces struck three Iranian oil tankers in response to a missile attack on Navy warships; at least five people were killed earlier in the week during a US bombardment of southern Iran, and the Trump administration is responding militarily while also targeting foreign financial institutions handling Iran's money.

#### Fuel tanker explosion in Iran kills 11, injures 7

**In short:** A fuel tanker exploded in western Iran late Saturday, killing 11 people and injuring seven others near a traffic police station close to the Kurdish city of Sanandaj, some 400 kilometres west of Tehran, state media reported. IRNA said the tanker hit other cars before bursting into flames, citing a faulty brake system; the injured were transferred to hospitals, and experts blame Iran's high road fatality rate of more than 17,000 deaths annually on unsafe vehicles, disregard for traffic rules and inadequate emergency services.

#### US ambassador calls violent Israeli settlers 'terrorists' after weeks of West Bank unrest

**In short:** US Ambassador to Israel Mike Huckabee condemned Israeli settler violence in the occupied West Bank on Saturday, calling behaviour including squatting, burning cars, torching mosques and vandalising homes "an act of terror" during a visit to Turmus Ayya, which has reported frequent settler attacks. Such attacks have surged since the Oct 7, 2023 Hamas-led attack on southern Israel, according to the UN humanitarian office; Huckabee also used the term "terrorists" last month after unrest in Qusra, and on Saturday referenced graffiti in Bazariya reading "Huckabee regards from the terrorists."

#### Mudslide kills 3, leaves 10 missing in China

**In short:** A mudslide killed three people and left 10 missing in Suichuan County, Jiangxi province in eastern China, damaging a dozen houses after days of torrential rain from tropical cyclone Saudel, state media said Sunday. Rescuers relocated residents to safer ground; separately, CCTV reported two people killed in Hunan province from downpours, and Xinhua reported another person killed by a landslide in a different Jiangxi village a day earlier.

#### Nepal floods: DNA testing offers last hope

**In short:** Amrit Diyali's family in Nepal is relying on DNA testing to identify whether a severed hand found downstream belongs to his younger brother Raj Kumar, missing with his wife and four-year-old son since devastating Aug 26 floods triggered by an avalanche near the Nepal-Tibet border. Authorities are using DNA testing to identify hundreds of bodies recovered after the Bhotekoshi River floodwaters killed more than 1,300 and left around 5,000 missing, with police collecting 2,272 DNA samples—1,260 from bodies and 1,012 from relatives.

#### South Korea pledges support for reconstruction

**In short:** Nepal Foreign Minister Shisir Khanal and South Korean counterpart Cho Hyun discussed ongoing search, rescue and relief operations following devastating floods that killed more than 1,300 and left around 5,000 missing, during a meeting in Kathmandu on Sunday. Khanal thanked South Korea for sending an inter-agency rapid response team, and the two held in-depth discussions on efforts to locate missing South Korean nationals.

#### Glaciers, Water Towers of the Planet Earth

**In short:** Vast regions of the Nepal-Tibet border were devastated by the collapse of Himalayan glaciers on August 26, with the death toll reaching around 1,500 and over 4,000 still missing. M.

Vast regions of the Nepal-Tibet border were devastated by the collapse of Himalayan glaciers on August 26, with the death toll reaching around 1,500 and over 4,000 still missing. M. A. Siraj explains that glaciers are dense ice bodies forming in cold regions where snow accumulates faster than it melts, hold about 69% of the world's freshwater, and cover roughly 10% of Earth's land; the Himalayan Hindu Kush region alone has an estimated 54,000 to 63,000 glaciers feeding at least 10 major Asian river systems. A glacier collapse is a sudden breakaway of ice and rock that can trigger destructive debris flows and GLOF (Glacial Lake Outburst Flood) events, as occurred in the Nepal-Tibet Himalaya last August.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Glaciers, Water Towers of the Planet Earth
- **Told:** Vast regions of the Nepal-Tibet border were devastated by the collapse of Himalayan glaciers on August 26, with the death toll reaching around 1,500 and over 4,000 still missing.
- **Purpose:** A glacier collapse is a sudden breakaway of ice and rock that can trigger destructive debris flows and GLOF (Glacial Lake Outburst Flood) events, as occurred in the Nepal-Tibet Himalaya last August.

**Important points**
- Siraj explains that glaciers are dense ice bodies forming in cold regions where snow accumulates faster than it melts, hold about 69% of the world's freshwater, and cover roughly 10% of Earth's land.
- the Himalayan Hindu Kush region alone has an estimated 54,000 to 63,000 glaciers feeding at least 10 major Asian river systems.
- A glacier collapse is a sudden breakaway of ice and rock that can trigger destructive debris flows and GLOF (Glacial Lake Outburst Flood) events, as occurred in the Nepal-Tibet Himalaya last August.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.10_

#### Rajnath Singh set for 3-day Sri Lanka visit to boost defence ties

**In short:** Defence Minister Rajnath Singh will visit Sri Lanka from September 8 to 10, the first visit by an Indian Defence Minister in 38 years since K.C. Pant in 1988, for talks with President Anura Kumara Dissanayake, Prime Minister Harini Amarasuriya and Defence Secretary Sampath Thuyacontha on defence cooperation including air-defence systems and implementation of the April 5, 2025 India-Sri Lanka Defence Cooperation MoU.

#### China teams scour ruins at flood-hit border crossing

**In short:** Chinese officials said on Sunday that workers recovered 12 more bodies as the search continued for hundreds still missing after a massive mudslide destroyed China's main border crossing with Nepal, opening devastated Gyirong County in Tibet to select media for the first time to show the scale of destruction at the destroyed immigration post. The mudslide has killed more than 1,300 people on both sides of the border with more than 5,000 missing; on the Chinese side 43 were confirmed dead and 519 remained missing as of Saturday, with recovery teams taking fingerprints and recording identifying features as families and diplomats privately expressed frustration over limited information-sharing.

Chinese officials said on Sunday that workers recovered 12 more bodies as the search continued for hundreds still missing after a massive mudslide destroyed China's main border crossing with Nepal, opening devastated Gyirong County in Tibet to select media for the first time to show the scale of destruction at the destroyed immigration post. The mudslide has killed more than 1,300 people on both sides of the border with more than 5,000 missing; on the Chinese side 43 were confirmed dead and 519 remained missing as of Saturday, with recovery teams taking fingerprints and recording identifying features as families and diplomats privately expressed frustration over limited information-sharing.

#### Bhutan leads Southeast Asia region in eliminating rabies

**In short:** Bhutan has become the first country in the WHO Southeast Asia region to eliminate dog-transmitted rabies as a public health problem, with WHO validating the achievement on Saturday and the kingdom's last rabies death recorded in June 2023. WHO Director-General Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus congratulated Bhutan as another step toward ending human deaths from dog-transmitted rabies by 2030, crediting a two-pronged strategy of dog vaccination and post-exposure prophylaxis, strong collaboration between health and agriculture ministries, and community engagement through De-suung volunteers.

#### Iran says it struck a U.S. vessel in Hormuz; Washington denies claim

**In short:** Iran said on Sunday it struck an unmanned U.S. vessel trying to enter the Strait of Hormuz, a claim the U.S.

Iran said on Sunday it struck an unmanned U.S. vessel trying to enter the Strait of Hormuz, a claim the U.S. military dismissed as a "total lie," a day after U.S. forces said they struck three Iranian oil tankers in response to Navy warships being targeted with ballistic missiles. Iran's Parliament Speaker Mohammad Bagher Ghalibaf warned that any further U.S. aggression would face "faster, more intense and more painful" responses, as on-and-off fighting has persisted since U.S. and Israeli attacks on Iran began on February 28 despite a fragile June ceasefire announcement.

**In short:** Zheng Qinwen won the last seven games to beat Madison Keys 1-6, 7-6 (3), 7-5 and reach the US Open last 16 on Saturday, saving a match point at 5-1 in the third set after coming through qualifying following elbow-surgery recovery. #### India end wait of two decades

**In short:** India qualified for the AFC U-20 Asian Cup main tournament for the first time in 20 years after a commanding 3-0 win over Bangladesh in their final group match in Tashkent on Sunday. The win took India to six points from three matches, and with Syria losing 0-3 to Uzbekistan later on Sunday, India finished second in Group B as one of the seven best runners-up teams across eight qualifying groups.

#### Duleep Trophy final: Kishan ton leads East's run feast vs South

**In short:** Skipper Ishan Kishan struck an unbeaten 111 (14 fours, 1 six) as East Zone reached 385 for four against South Zone on the opening day of the Duleep Trophy final in Chennai on Sunday, with Kumar Kushagra 63 not out and a fifth-wicket stand of 144 in 26.4 overs. Other contributors were Vaibhav Sooryavanshi 44, Abhimanyu Easwaran 72 and Shikhar Mohan 85; Kishan completed his 10th first-class hundred and 4,000 first-class runs, while Mohammad Siraj went 14-1-96-0 as South struggled on a true pitch.

#### Antonelli dazzles to Italian GP victory

**In short:** Kimi Antonelli won the Italian Grand Prix at Monza on Sunday with a charge from 19th on the grid—after exceeding permitted power-unit changes—to beat Mercedes teammate George Russell by 3.857 seconds, with Max Verstappen third, Lando Norris fourth, Oscar Piastri fifth and Lewis Hamilton sixth after Charles Leclerc crashed out triggering an early red flag. Antonelli's seventh win of the season and first home GP victory stretched his drivers' championship lead to 66 points over Russell (267 to 201), with Mercedes leading the constructors' standings on 468 points.

#### Junior Asia Cup hockey: India rally to draw with Japan

**In short:** The Indian junior men's hockey team rallied to secure a 3-3 draw against Japan in their second Pool A match of the Junior Asia Cup in Moqi, China, on Sunday after leading 2-1 at half-time through penalty stroke and penalty corner goals. Japan levelled through Kazuki Nakanishi in the 48th minute and went ahead 3-2 in the 59th minute before captain Anmol Ekka converted a penalty corner with 59 seconds remaining to make it 3-3; India will next face Korea on Tuesday.

#### We plan together: Charani on India's spin strategy in Pak rout

**In short:** Young left-arm spinner Shree Charani, who took 3/7 as India bowled Pakistan out for their lowest-ever T20I total of 55 in a seven-wicket Women's T20 Asia Cup win in Dubai on Saturday, said slow bowlers map out strategy together before matches. Charani, Prema Rawat (3/9) and Deepti Sharma (2/5) combined to choke Pakistan's batting; India finished Group A on top with six points after winning all three games, and Charani is one wicket away from 50 T20I wickets.

#### Qinwen in big US Open comeback

**In short:** Olympic gold medalist Zheng Qinwen rallied from a set down to beat No. 22 Madison Keys 1-6, 7-6 (3), 7-5 at the U.S.

Olympic gold medalist Zheng Qinwen rallied from a set down to beat No. 22 Madison Keys 1-6, 7-6 (3), 7-5 at the U.S. Open in New York on Saturday, winning the final seven games after Keys failed to convert a match point at 5-1 in the third set. No. 13 Naomi Osaka also advanced to the fourth round defeating Elise Mertens 6-3, 3-6, 7-5; No. 9 Taylor Fritz blew a two-set lead and lost to No. 24 Francisco Cerundolo, No. 10 Amanda Anisimova fell to No. 24 Anastasia Potapova, and 2023 champion Coco Gauff beat Cristina Bucsa 6-3, 6-4 to set up a quarterfinal meeting with 18-year-old Iva Jovic.

#### Italian GP: Gasly stuns Monza again with first-ever pole

**In short:** Alpine's Pierre Gasly claimed his first-ever Formula 1 pole position for the Italian Grand Prix at Monza on Saturday, finishing 0.060 seconds ahead of Mercedes' George Russell. Oscar Piastri was third-fastest but received a three-place grid penalty for impeding Red Bull's Liam Lawson in Q2, promoting Charles Leclerc and Lewis Hamilton to the second row; championship leader Kimi Antonelli qualified seventh but starts at the back due to a planned engine change, while Gasly's 2020 Monza win remains his only F1 victory.

#### 18 centuries: tracing Smriti Mandhana's journey to the top

**In short:** Smriti Mandhana's 18th international century—a 124 off 57 balls against Hong Kong at the Women's T20 Asia Cup in Dubai on September 3—made her the highest run-scorer in women's international cricket with 10,880 runs, surpassing Mithali Raj's 10,868, and also broke the record for most centuries (18), passing Meg Lanning and Laura Wolvaardt; ten of her 18 hundreds have come in the last two years at a higher strike rate.

#### We are a very good competitor for the Asian Games gold: coach Marijne

**In short:** Indian women's hockey head coach Sjoerd Marijne said in Bengaluru that India is a realistic contender for Asian Games gold—which would also secure direct qualification for the 2028 Los Angeles Olympics—after a historic fifth place at the 2026 FIH World Cup, with World No. 8 India facing No.

Indian women's hockey head coach Sjoerd Marijne said in Bengaluru that India is a realistic contender for Asian Games gold—which would also secure direct qualification for the 2028 Los Angeles Olympics—after a historic fifth place at the 2026 FIH World Cup, with World No. 8 India facing No. 4 China, hosts Japan (No. 11) and Korea (No. 17). Marijne said India drew China at the World Cup but could have won, beat Japan 2-1 in the Nations Cup, and that the team is "90% about ourselves and 10% about the opponent" while acknowledging penalty-corner and field-goal conversion as areas to keep pushing in close big-match contests.

### Lifestyle

#### Beyond recovery

**In short:** Ahead of World Physiotherapy Day on September 8, physiotherapy is increasingly moving beyond treating injuries to preventive care and rehabilitation across life stages, with specialists supporting cardiac recovery, women's health through pregnancy and postpartum, early intervention for children with developmental concerns, geriatric mobility, and sports injury prevention. Cardiovascular physiotherapist Dr Shraddha Patel emphasises heart and lung training after surgery, women's health physiotherapist Dr Sanjana Venkataraman highlights care from conception through childbirth, paediatric neuro physiotherapist Dr Aishwarya Talgeri stresses early milestone intervention, and sports physiotherapist Dr Vaibhavi Pathade notes more young people now seek physiotherapy before injury rather than only after.

#### Monsoon barrier damage

**In short:** Excessive monsoon moisture, sweat, fungal growth and over-cleansing can disrupt the skin barrier, causing dryness, sensitivity, redness, breakouts, itching, rough texture and slow healing. Ayurvedic remedies suggested include aloe vera gel for hydration, rose water as a toner, oatmeal and honey masks, sandalwood paste with rose water, and turmeric with yogurt or honey, alongside lifestyle tips such as staying hydrated and eating antioxidant-rich seasonal fruits and vegetables.

#### DHUMAPANA THERAPY OR TREND?

**In short:** Medicated Ayurvedic smoking, or Dhumapana—the controlled inhalation of smoke from specific medicinal herbs—gained attention after lifestyle creator Shenaz Treasury shared undergoing the physician-prescribed treatment at an Ayurveda clinic, though experts stress it differs from cigarette smoking. Dr Anjana Kalia and Dr Neeru Jain explain it is a traditional, supervised therapeutic procedure described in texts such as Charaka Samhita for head and upper respiratory disorders, but warn smoke and fine particles may irritate the respiratory tract, herbal cigarettes are not equivalent, and more clinical research is needed on benefits and long-term safety.

- **Told:** Medicated Ayurvedic smoking, or Dhumapana—the controlled inhalation of smoke from specific medicinal herbs—gained attention after lifestyle creator Shenaz Treasury shared undergoing the physician-prescribed treatment at an Ayurveda clinic, though experts stress it differs from cigarette smoking.
- **Purpose:** Dr Anjana Kalia and Dr Neeru Jain explain it is a traditional, supervised therapeutic procedure described in texts such as Charaka Samhita for head and upper respiratory disorders, but warn smoke and fine particles may irritate the respiratory tract, herbal cigarettes are not equivalent, and more clinical research is needed on benefits and long-term safety.

**Important points**
- Medicated Ayurvedic smoking, or Dhumapana—the controlled inhalation of smoke from specific medicinal herbs—gained attention after lifestyle creator Shenaz Treasury shared undergoing the physician-prescribed treatment at an Ayurveda clinic, though experts stress it differs from cigarette smoking. Dr Anjana Kalia and Dr Neeru Jain explain it is a traditional, supervised therapeutic procedure described in texts such as Charaka Samhita for head and upper respiratory disorders, but warn smoke and fine particles may irritate the respiratory tract, herbal cigarettes are not equivalent, and more clinical research is needed on benefits and long-term safety.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.8_

#### KAKAPO — The fattest, heaviest, flightless Parrots

**In short:** The kakapo, the world's heaviest flightless parrot weighing up to 4 kilograms, is found in New Zealand's protected island sanctuaries and can live 60 to 90 years. Nearly extinct by the 1900s after predators such as cats and stoats were introduced, a Department of Conservation recovery programme launched in 1995 when only 51 remained has raised numbers to over 320; this year a bountiful rimu berry harvest prompted a record breeding season, with the youngest 2026 chick, female Awarua-A3, hatching on March 30.

#### 20 Weird English Idioms Explained

**In short:** The page lists and explains 20 common English idioms and their meanings, including "back to square one" (return to the beginning), "bark up the wrong tree" (pursue the wrong idea), "beat a dead horse" (waste effort on a pointless thing), "catch-22" (no-win situation), "dark horse" (unexpected winner), "devil's advocate" (argue the opposite side), "dog-eat-dog" (ruthlessly competitive), "elephant in the room" (obvious issues everyone avoids), and "face the music" (face the consequences).

#### Rama answers Vaali

**In short:** A dying Vaali questions Rama's righteousness, his enmity with Vaali and why He had to kill him; Navalpakkam Vasudevachariar recounts that Rama answers every objection, telling Vaali he ignored conventions, failed to consult intellectual superiors and acted as he pleased. Rama says all earth—including animals, birds and trees—is under Ikshvaku control, that as Bharata's representative enforcing moral rule He must punish Vaali for misbehaving with Sugreeva's wife as with a daughter-in-law, and that He cannot spare one who has sinned and broken all rules.

#### Balanced bond portfolio blueprint

**In short:** Vaishali R. Venkat outlines how to build a bond portfolio that can withstand defaults, changing interest rates and shifting economic conditions, starting with clarifying when and how much money is needed, desired income versus capital appreciation, and risk tolerance.

Vaishali R. Venkat outlines how to build a bond portfolio that can withstand defaults, changing interest rates and shifting economic conditions, starting with clarifying when and how much money is needed, desired income versus capital appreciation, and risk tolerance. She advises mixing government securities, Treasury bonds, corporate bonds and bank bonds across issuers, sectors and maturities; scrutinising credit ratings without treating them as guarantees; and rebalancing at least every six months to a year while tracking RBI announcements, issuer developments and macro indicators such as GDP growth, inflation and CPI.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Balanced bond portfolio blueprint
- **Told:** Vaishali R.
- **Purpose:** She advises mixing government securities, Treasury bonds, corporate bonds and bank bonds across issuers, sectors and maturities; scrutinising credit ratings without treating them as guarantees; and rebalancing at least every six months to a year while tracking RBI announcements, issuer developments and macro indicators such as GDP growth, inflation and CPI.

**Important points**
- She advises mixing government securities, Treasury bonds, corporate bonds and bank bonds across issuers, sectors and maturities.
- scrutinising credit ratings without treating them as guarantees.
- and rebalancing at least every six months to a year while tracking RBI announcements, issuer developments and macro indicators such as GDP growth, inflation and CPI.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.11_

#### Buying insurance at discount? Think twice

**In short:** K. Nitya Kalyani warns that in insurance the cheapest option is not necessarily the best value, because a lower premium often reflects a changed bundle of risks, conditions, exclusions and benefits rather than the same cover at a better price.

K. Nitya Kalyani warns that in insurance the cheapest option is not necessarily the best value, because a lower premium often reflects a changed bundle of risks, conditions, exclusions and benefits rather than the same cover at a better price. She cites examples including a discounted motor policy restricting repairs to non-network garages, a home fire policy discount that removed flood cover before IRDAI mandated bundled flood cover from 2021, and windshield claims that can cost more in lost no-claim bonus than the repair itself, urging buyers to ask what add-ons, deductibles, co-pay, cashless restrictions or repairer limits have changed before accepting a discount.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Buying insurance at discount? Think twice
- **Told:** K.
- **Purpose:** She cites examples including a discounted motor policy restricting repairs to non-network garages, a home fire policy discount that removed flood cover before IRDAI mandated bundled flood cover from 2021, and windshield claims that can cost more in lost no-claim bonus than the repair itself, urging buyers to ask what add-ons, deductibles, co-pay, cashless restrictions or repairer limits have changed before accepting a discount.

**Important points**
- She cites examples including a discounted motor policy restricting repairs to non-network garages, a home fire policy discount that removed flood cover before IRDAI mandated bundled flood cover from 2021, and windshield claims that can cost more in lost no-claim bonus than the repair itself, urging buyers to ask what add-ons, deductibles, co-pay, cashless restrictions or repairer limits have changed before accepting a discount.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.11_

#### Getting him to open up is like pulling teeth

**In short:** S. Upendran’s Know Your English column explains that “like pulling teeth” means a slow, painful process—whether losing weight, conversing with someone reticent, or waking early—and distinguishes “parroting someone” (mindless repetition without understanding) from “aping someone” (clumsily copying another’s mannerisms, style, or behaviour).

### Other

#### Scientists plan to find out if there is alien debris on the Moon

**In short:** A SETI Institute–led team proposes that microscopic particles shed by alien civilisations may have accumulated on the lunar surface, making the moon a possible archive of extraterrestrial technology. Modelling how such "Arkhipov particles" and operational "Bracewell particles" could reach the solar system, the researchers suggest a mission could sift about a cubic metre of lunar soil for artificial grains, though distinguishing alien debris from human spacecraft litter would require chemical analysis.

#### Sulphuric acid: not so deadly

**In short:** Researchers report in Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences on September 4 that certain peptides remain stable for weeks at room temperature in 98% concentrated sulphuric acid and fold into omega-loop shapes, using acid molecules as scaffolding—a first for a biological macromolecule in that solvent, after earlier work on nucleic acid bases and amino acids. Because hydrolysis needs water, the near-waterless acid does not break peptide bonds; the findings challenge assumptions that Venus’s sulphuric-acid clouds are too harsh for complex chemistry relevant to the search for life beyond Earth.

Because hydrolysis needs water, the near-waterless acid does not break peptide bonds; the findings challenge assumptions that Venus’s sulphuric-acid clouds are too harsh for complex chemistry relevant to the search for life beyond Earth.

**What this is**

## Technology

### Top 5 — Technology

#### 1. Proposed Karnataka AI University to begin skill programmes before full-fledged campus takes shape

**In short:** Karnataka plans to start AI education and skill-development initiatives before the proposed Karnataka Artificial Intelligence University is fully operational, with the advisory committee’s first meeting in Bengaluru on Saturday under Infosys co-founder Kris Gopalakrishnan and IT-BT and Science and Technology Department secretary Dr N Manjula as co-chair discussing certificate, diploma, reskilling and executive education programmes. Programmes will focus on generative and agentic AI, data analytics, cloud computing, cybersecurity, AI infrastructure and data centres, physical AI, semiconductor and hardware applications, and AI literacy, targeting university students and graduates, working technology professionals, other-sector professionals, and rural youth.

Karnataka plans to start AI education and skill-development initiatives before the proposed Karnataka Artificial Intelligence University is fully operational, with the advisory committee’s first meeting in Bengaluru on Saturday under Infosys co-founder Kris Gopalakrishnan and IT-BT and Science and Technology Department secretary Dr N Manjula as co-chair discussing certificate, diploma, reskilling and executive education programmes. Programmes will focus on generative and agentic AI, data analytics, cloud computing, cybersecurity, AI infrastructure and data centres, physical AI, semiconductor and hardware applications, and AI literacy, targeting university students and graduates, working technology professionals, other-sector professionals, and rural youth. The committee stressed measuring success by jobs created, entrepreneurship and economic opportunities, using existing educational infrastructure and AI learning labs beyond Bengaluru, with industry partners contributing faculty, mentors, certification and real-world projects; Gopalakrishnan said the immediate task is combining industry expertise and academic infrastructure, while Dr Manjula said an institutional framework will coordinate stakeholders and validate curricula as the legislative process proceeds.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Proposed Karnataka AI University to begin skill programmes before full-fledged campus takes shape
- **Told:** Karnataka plans to start AI education and skill-development initiatives before the proposed Karnataka Artificial Intelligence University is fully operational, with the advisory committee’s first meeting in Bengaluru on Saturday under Infosys co-founder Kris Gopalakrishnan and IT-BT and Science and Technology Department secretary Dr N Manjula as co-chair discussing certificate, diploma, reskilling and executive education programmes.
- **Purpose:** The committee stressed measuring success by jobs created, entrepreneurship and economic opportunities, using existing educational infrastructure and AI learning labs beyond Bengaluru, with industry partners contributing faculty, mentors, certification and real-world projects; Gopalakrishnan said the immediate task is combining industry expertise and academic infrastructure, while Dr Manjula said an institutional framework will coordinate stakeholders and validate curricula as the legislative process proceeds.

**Important points**
- The committee stressed measuring success by jobs created, entrepreneurship and economic opportunities, using existing educational infrastructure and AI learning labs beyond Bengaluru, with industry partners contributing faculty, mentors, certification and real-world projects.
- Gopalakrishnan said the immediate task is combining industry expertise and academic infrastructure, while Dr Manjula said an institutional framework will coordinate stakeholders and validate curricula as the legislative process proceeds.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.2_

#### 2. No 'space' for job loss, say ISRO staff amid privatisation moves

**In short:** Two days after the Indian Space Research Organisation's Staff Association wrote to chairperson V Narayanan expressing concerns over the organisation's privatisation plans, members clarified they are not against private players entering the space industry but want ISRO to clearly communicate its future direction to employees. On 6 September an office bearer told The Print that if the government outsources tasks such as rocket building and technology development to private companies, staff need clarity on ISRO's role; on 4 September the association sent a five-page letter citing IN-SPACe chairperson Pawan Goenka's comments that ISRO will no longer make or manufacture launch vehicles, work that will go to the private sector or PSUs.

Two days after the Indian Space Research Organisation's Staff Association wrote to chairperson V Narayanan expressing concerns over the organisation's privatisation plans, members clarified they are not against private players entering the space industry but want ISRO to clearly communicate its future direction to employees. On 6 September an office bearer told The Print that if the government outsources tasks such as rocket building and technology development to private companies, staff need clarity on ISRO's role; on 4 September the association sent a five-page letter citing IN-SPACe chairperson Pawan Goenka's comments that ISRO will no longer make or manufacture launch vehicles, work that will go to the private sector or PSUs. With no official communication on transferring launch vehicle design and manufacturing, a VSSC office bearer said at least 50 per cent of the workforce could lose jobs if the private sector takes over, though employees are not completely hostile to privatisation if integration preserves the relevance of existing staff and centres.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** No 'space' for job loss, say ISRO staff amid privatisation moves
- **Told:** Two days after the Indian Space Research Organisation's Staff Association wrote to chairperson V Narayanan expressing concerns over the organisation's privatisation plans, members clarified they are not against private players entering the space industry but want ISRO to clearly communicate its future direction to employees.
- **Purpose:** With no official communication on transferring launch vehicle design and manufacturing, a VSSC office bearer said at least 50 per cent of the workforce could lose jobs if the private sector takes over, though employees are not completely hostile to privatisation if integration preserves the relevance of existing staff and centres.

**Important points**
- With no official communication on transferring launch vehicle design and manufacturing, a VSSC office bearer said at least 50 per cent of the workforce could lose jobs if the private sector takes over, though employees are not completely hostile to privatisation if integration preserves the relevance of existing staff and centres.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.1_

#### 3. ISRO's role is by no means diminishing: space officials

**In short:** IN-SPACe chairman Pawan Kumar Goenka and ISRO refuted suggestions that privatisation threatens ISRO's workforce, responding to a September 4 joint letter from nine ISRO employee associations citing Goenka's August 21 remarks that ISRO would eventually not make or manufacture launch vehicles. Goenka said ISRO's role is "by no means diminishing" and it will remain the bedrock of the Indian space sector, concentrating on advanced R&D, scientific and strategic missions and infrastructure including the Bharatiya Antariksh Station by 2035 and a crewed lunar mission by 2040, while industry scales mature launch vehicles and satellites; ISRO called privatisation suggestions "completely baseless and incorrect."

#### 4. CM seeks deeper Indo-US collaboration in tech, edu, research

**In short:** Chief Minister D K Shivakumar on Sunday sought deeper India–US collaboration in technology, education, innovation, research, investment and culture at an event marking the 250th anniversary of American independence, noting over 1,000 American companies operate in Karnataka—nearly half of total US corporate presence in India—and over 80,000 American citizens live in Bengaluru. He said Karnataka is developing KWIN City, a Semiconductor Park and an AI Tech Park including an AI Hub and India's first Public Sector AI University, with USA's Ambassador to India Sergio Gor present.

#### 5. Isro rules out privatisation, says role will 'progressively strengthen'

**In short:** ISRO clarified on Sunday it would neither be privatised nor diminished, saying private-sector participation lets it focus on frontier R&D, human spaceflight, next-generation launch systems and deep-space missions while industry scales mature technologies. The statement followed nine employee associations seeking clarity on September 4 about transferring manufacturing and operations to private entities, referencing IN-SPACe chief Pawan Goenka's August 21 remarks; Goenka said ISRO remains the bedrock of India's space sector and reforms since 2020 aim to expand the ecosystem.

### More technology

#### 'India must look beyond unicorns to measure startup success'

**In short:** KPMG India Partner Akhilesh Tuteja told DH that India's startup ecosystem is diffusing innovation beyond consumer apps into agriculture, energy, smart manufacturing and space, while investor attention is shifting toward AI, semiconductors, infrastructure and industry-specific applications with greater scrutiny of profitability and burn rates. India's VC exit value in Q4 2025 reached its second-highest level on record, with an average of 16 new aerospace and defence startups a year since 2020 and $152 million in sector funding in 2024 alone.

KPMG India Partner Akhilesh Tuteja told DH that India's startup ecosystem is diffusing innovation beyond consumer apps into agriculture, energy, smart manufacturing and space, while investor attention is shifting toward AI, semiconductors, infrastructure and industry-specific applications with greater scrutiny of profitability and burn rates. India's VC exit value in Q4 2025 reached its second-highest level on record, with an average of 16 new aerospace and defence startups a year since 2020 and $152 million in sector funding in 2024 alone. Tuteja said unicorn counts measure valuation rather than impact, and that entrepreneurs created, people impacted, jobs generated, per-capita income and unemployment are more meaningful success metrics for India.

#### Sharpest pictures of sun’s surface reveal previously unseen whirlpools

**In short:** Scientists using the NSF Daniel K. Inouye Solar Telescope (DKIST) in Hawaii—the world’s largest solar telescope, with a four-metre primary mirror—have captured the highest-resolution images yet of the sun’s photosphere, revealing small plasma coils at granule edges and fast-spinning vortices that grow up to 2.7 times their size in 18 seconds and rotate at 1.6–2.8 km/s.

Scientists using the NSF Daniel K. Inouye Solar Telescope (DKIST) in Hawaii—the world’s largest solar telescope, with a four-metre primary mirror—have captured the highest-resolution images yet of the sun’s photosphere, revealing small plasma coils at granule edges and fast-spinning vortices that grow up to 2.7 times their size in 18 seconds and rotate at 1.6–2.8 km/s. Published in Nature on August 5, the findings suggest these whirlpools efficiently transport energy and magnetic flux, braid magnetic fields at small scales, and may help explain solar flares and the unexpectedly hot outer atmosphere.

**What this is**

#### Studies show glaciers are shrinking

**In short:** Nazeer Waris writes that mounting evidence shows global glaciers are shrinking as warming accelerates melting beyond natural rates. NASA GRACE satellite data from 2002 to 2020 shows Antarctica lost around 150 gigatons of ice annually, contributing 0.4 millimetres of global sea-level rise per year; ice caps in the Canadian Arctic have shrunk 50% in the last century, and ICIMOD's March 2026 Hindu Kush Himalaya Glacier Outlook found ice loss rates across the region have doubled since 2000 despite dangerously under-researched coverage of only around 38 glaciers with long-term monitoring on 21.

#### Frontier professionals: The new AI-first force that redesigns work

**In short:** Microsoft's 2026 Work Trend Index finds one in three Indian workers are "frontier professionals" who redesign multi-step workflows around AI agents, the highest share globally at 16%, with 78% of India's AI users saying AI enables work that was impossible a year ago. Industry leaders say these workers combine AI fluency, domain expertise and human judgement to drive productivity gains and typically command premium salaries as organisations move from experimentation to practical adoption.

#### Why Australia's music industry banned AI music in chart listing

**In short:** Sahana Venugopal reports that on August 25 Australia’s ARIA barred wholly AI-generated songs from its charts while allowing recordings that are “substantially human made,” after an undisclosed AI remix of Madonna’s “Like a Prayer” by Josh Fawaz ranked second on ARIA’s top 20 Australian singles chart and drew over 23 million YouTube views. The piece notes ARIA may reject or remove ineligible tracks from charts and awards, that publishers are suing AI platforms over copyrighted training data—including a landmark $1.5 billion Anthropic settlement—and that Spotify will soon label “AI Persona” artists as listeners demand clearer differentiation and filtering of AI-assisted music.
